**Goal.** Every read of memories/themes/observations/watch_items/distillations/OOB goes through the join. Behavior identical (mediator's primary topic is always `relationship`).

This is a **full data-access audit sprint**, not a query helper refactor. Direct reads in hot context + read tools + OOB check + cross-thread privacy + decay + background summaries all in scope.

## S1 + S2a + S2b lessons the executor must honor

1. **CLI scripts**: invoke with `--help` to confirm parseable.
2. **SQL fragments in Python strings**: dry-run via `EXPLAIN` or sanity-check types (no `max(uuid)` etc.).
3. **asyncpg pool against `DATABASE_URL`**: pass `statement_cache_size=0` (Supabase port 6543 is transaction-mode).
4. **No `hash()` for deterministic IDs** — use `hashlib.sha1`.
5. **Schema filters in validation queries** use `'mediator'` not `'public'`.
6. **No scope creep**: don't add unrelated features or unused fields to shared classes.
7. **S2b dual-key code is GONE**: the old `OR bot_id IS NULL` filter no longer applies. New code uses pure `bot_id = ctx.bot_id` filtering.
8. **For psql migrations against Supabase**: use port 5432 session-mode for DDL, not 6543 transaction-mode.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — artifact_topics JOIN cutover for reads

## Overview

Sprint 1 built the `artifact_topics` polymorphic join table and backfilled ~235 active rows. Sprint 2a stamped insert sites so every new artifact ships with a matching `artifact_topics` row, and Sprint 2b retired the dual-key fallback paths so reads now use pure `bot_id = ctx.bot_id` filtering with NOT NULL constraints on scope columns.

S3 cuts every artifact **read** over to the join: instead of reading directly from `memories | themes | observations | watch_items | distillations | out_of_bounds`, code joins through `artifact_topics` filtered by `topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id AND status = 'active'`. Behavior is identical at launch — mediator's `primary_topic_id` is always the `relationship` topic — but the read path is now scope-aware, which is the foundation S4+ needs.

Locked decisions from the brief (do not relitigate):
1. Helper `app/services/topic_filter.py::join_artifact_topics(table_alias, topic_id_param)` returns a SQL JOIN fragment.
2. `decay.py` is **per-topic** (per-job, per-bot decision; per-topic policy chosen so a coach's career observations don't decay because a mediator hasn't reinforced them).
3. OOB delivery-time guardrail filters via `artifact_topics.topic_id = ctx.primary_topic_id`. Global OOB (no artifact_topics row) stays as the no-row mode and is unused at launch.
4. Mediator primary topic is **always** `relationship` — every existing read site keyed off mediator behavior gets that `topic_id` by default.
5. No multi-topic writes (S6), no scope enforcement beyond hardcoded primary (S4), no `scope` argument on read tools (S4).

Constraints from CLAUDE-level guidance:
- Dirty pre-existing files that are **off-limits**: `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`, `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`. Property test (work item 6) therefore lives in a **new** test file rather than extending `test_hot_context.py`.
- Dirty files where edits are **allowed for S3 only** because they're primary work sites: `app/services/hot_context.py`, `app/services/prompts.py`, `app/services/time_context.py` — only for query rewrites, no piling-on.
- Branch already exists: `s3-join-cutover` at HEAD `13fb67e`. Do NOT modify `docs/multi-agent-architecture.md`.
- S2b lesson: legacy `OR bot_id IS NULL` dual-key filter is gone. New code uses pure equality.

## Phase 1: Helper, plumbing, and pre-flight

### Step 1: Confirm preconditions (`scripts/`, `migrations/validation/`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** S2a/S2b production state with a one-shot query: `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics WHERE status='active'` (expect ≥235 — S1 backfilled 235, S2a/S2b may have added more). Use the `DATABASE_URL` env with `statement_cache_size=0` (Supabase port 6543 transaction-mode) per S1 lesson #3.
2. **Verify** the `idx_artifact_topics_topic_status` index from `migrations/0021_artifact_topics.sql:41-42` is present, plus `artifact_topics_active_artifact_key` partial unique index on `(artifact_table, artifact_id) WHERE status='active'` from `:28-30`.
3. **Decide** (per question to user) whether to add a new partial composite index `(topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active'` — the brief mentions this exact shape for the EXPLAIN check but it doesn't currently exist. Default assumption below: add it via `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql` because the existing `(topic_id, status)` non-partial index is wider than the read path needs and the brief explicitly calls out this index in the EXPLAIN gate.

### Step 2: Add the topic-filter helper (`app/services/topic_filter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** the new module with a single function:
   ```python
   def join_artifact_topics(table_alias: str, topic_id_param: str = "$1") -> str:
       """Return a SQL JOIN fragment scoping <table_alias>.id by topic.

       The caller is responsible for passing the topic_id value at the
       parameter position named in `topic_id_param`. Caller must also
       define the artifact_table literal — this helper hardcodes it from
       the alias-to-table mapping below.
       """
   ```
   The function returns a fragment of the form:
   ```sql
   JOIN artifact_topics _at_<alias>
     ON _at_<alias>.artifact_table = '<table>'
    AND _at_<alias>.artifact_id   = <alias>.id
    AND _at_<alias>.topic_id      = <topic_id_param>
    AND _at_<alias>.status        = 'active'
   ```
2. **Encode** the alias-to-table map inside the helper for the six artifact tables (`memories`, `themes`, `observations`, `watch_items`, `distillations`, `out_of_bounds`). Raise `ValueError` for unknown aliases — the helper must fail loudly rather than silently emit an unscoped join.
3. **Document** in a one-line module docstring that the caller binds the topic UUID at the listed parameter index. No comments beyond the docstring + raise message (per session guidance).
4. **Add** a tiny unit test `tests/test_topic_filter.py` confirming the fragment shape for each of the six tables and the `ValueError` path. Cheap, runs in <1s.

### Step 3: Plumb `primary_topic_id` to `build_hot_context` (`app/services/hot_context.py`, callers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Inspect** the current signature `build_hot_context(pool, user, partner, triggering_message_ids, trigger_metadata)` at `app/services/hot_context.py:173-180`. None of the existing parameters carry topic context.
2. **Add** a keyword-only `primary_topic_id: UUID` parameter (required, no default — surface miswiring early per S2a lesson #6 "no scope creep, no defaults").
3. **Find** call sites: ripgrep `build_hot_context(` across `app/`. Expected hits: agentic loop in `app/services/agentic.py` (passes `ctx`). Pass `primary_topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
4. **Reject** None at the call boundary — raise `ValueError("build_hot_context requires primary_topic_id (S3 join cutover)")` if None is passed. This catches the lint-bypassed regression where someone forgets to populate scope.

## Phase 2: Read-site rewrites (in the brief's required order)

### Step 4: Rewrite `hot_context.py` reads (`app/services/hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Rewrite** the six SELECTs to JOIN `artifact_topics`. Citations + topic param indices:
   - `out_of_bounds` at `hot_context.py:228-233` → add helper-emitted JOIN; topic_id is a new bind, currently `$1` is `[user.id, partner.id]` uuid array — restructure params so topic_id is `$1` and the existing array slides to `$2`.
   - `memories` at `:249-256` → add JOIN; remove the `OR about_user_id IS NULL` only if test fixtures support it. The brief says S2b retired `bot_id IS NULL` filters, but this is `about_user_id IS NULL` (couple-level memories) — that's a content concern, not a scope concern. **Keep** the `OR about_user_id IS NULL` clause.
   - `themes` at `:274-281` → add JOIN.
   - `watch_items` at `:293-298` → add JOIN.
   - `observations` at `:316-326` → add JOIN.
   - `distillations` at `:345-358` → add JOIN.
2. **Use** the helper for every JOIN fragment; do not inline raw SQL. This is what the lint will enforce.
3. **Preserve** every existing `ORDER BY`, `LIMIT`, and additional filter (e.g., `status='active'`, `significance >= 3`, sharing-default downstream filtering). The join cutover must be **semantically invisible** at launch — that's what the property test in Step 8 will catch.
4. **Bind** `primary_topic_id` as the first parameter in each query for consistent positional shape.

### Step 5: Rewrite `read_tools.py` reads (`app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Rewrite** each artifact-table SELECT using the helper:
   - `list_themes` at `:520-531` (`FROM themes`)
   - `get_theme` at `:537-545` (`FROM themes WHERE id = $1`) — the JOIN doubles as scope-existence check.
   - `get_theme` related-memory lookup at `:549-552` (`SELECT id FROM memories WHERE $1 = ANY(...)`) — add JOIN.
   - `get_theme` related-observation lookup at `:553-556` — add JOIN.
   - `get_memories` at `:582-591` — add JOIN.
   - `list_watch_items` at `:609-619` — add JOIN.
   - `get_observations` at `:636-650` — add JOIN.
   - `get_distillations` at `:682-701` — add JOIN.
   - `get_oob` at `:739-748` — add JOIN.
2. **Pass** `ctx.primary_topic_id` to each helper invocation. All read tools receive `ctx: TurnContext`, which already carries `primary_topic_id` from S1 (`app/services/turn_context.py:28`).
3. **Adjust** dynamic `${len(params)}` numbering carefully — these queries build params incrementally. Pre-pend `primary_topic_id` and adjust all subsequent index expressions, OR bind topic at the **end** to avoid the reshuffling. Recommend: bind topic last per query, append it to `params` just before the LIMIT, and reference it as the explicit param index in the helper call. This keeps existing parameter ordering stable and minimizes diff churn.
4. **Keep** all secondary filters (sharing-default visibility, `target_user_id` checks for bridge candidates) intact.

     ```sql
     UPDATE themes

     FROM artifact_topics at

       AND at.topic_id       = $2
     ```

**Scope:** Medium

**Scope:** Medium

**Scope:** Small

3. **Implement** with the same approach as `lint_inserts.py`: walk `.py`, find `FROM <table>` line, extract enclosing triple-quoted SQL, check for `JOIN artifact_topics` substring. Non-zero exit on violations. Support `--help` and `--dir` flags for consistency.
4. **Wire** into pytest as a `test_lint_artifact_reads_clean` test that invokes the script as a subprocess and asserts exit code 0 (matches the existing pattern for `lint_inserts.py`; check `tests/test_ops_scripts.py` or similar).

1. Brief explicitly skips the eval-baseline re-run (eval framework still doesn't run cleanly against Supabase pooler — same posture as S2a). Capture an informal hot-context render snapshot only if time permits; otherwise defer to post-S4.

## Execution Order
1. Confirm preconditions (Step 1), then add the helper (Step 2) and plumb `primary_topic_id` (Step 3). These gate every subsequent rewrite.
2. Rewrite reads in the brief's required order: `hot_context.py` (Step 4), `read_tools.py` (Step 5), `oob_check.py` + `cross_thread_privacy.py` audit (Step 6), `scheduled_job_handlers.py` + `decay.py` + `withheld_reviews.py` audit (Step 7).
3. Land the property test (Step 8), EXPLAIN script (Step 9), and lint script (Step 10) once the rewrites compile.
4. Add the decay-scope test (Step 11) after `decay.py` is rewritten.
5. Validation in Steps 12–14.

## Validation Order
1. **Unit-level first**: topic-filter helper test, decay scope test — runs in seconds.
2. **Integration-level**: full mediator pytest, lint-clean check on `app/`.
3. **Property test**: hot-context frozen-fixture diff (work item 6).
4. **EXPLAIN gate** against prod (work item 4).
5. **Lint gate**: `lint_artifact_reads.py` exits 0 (work item 7).

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]

      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 5,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-011",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-019",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-026",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-031",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2b-constraints"
        ]
      }

        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

```
1	BEGIN;
2	
3	-- ============================================================
4	-- Sprint 1: artifact_topics — polymorphic join table linking artifacts to topics
5	-- ============================================================
6	
7	-- Polymorphic join: (artifact_table, artifact_id) identifies the artifact row.
8	-- No FK to individual artifact tables — orphan prevention is application
9	-- discipline; lint catches it in S2a.
10	CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS artifact_topics (
11	    artifact_table text NOT NULL,
12	    artifact_id uuid NOT NULL,
13	    topic_id uuid NOT NULL REFERENCES topics(id),
14	    status text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'active' CHECK (status IN ('active', 'retired')),
15	    tagged_by_bot_id text REFERENCES bots(id),
16	    reason text,
17	    created_at timestamptz NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
18	    retired_at timestamptz,
19	    PRIMARY KEY (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)
20	);
21	
22	-- ============================================================
23	-- Partial unique indexes — enforce at most one active row per (artifact, topic).
24	-- Retired rows coexist freely (historical record).
25	-- ============================================================
26	
27	-- Prevent duplicate active assignments for the same (artifact_table, artifact_id)
28	CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS artifact_topics_active_artifact_key
29	    ON artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id)
30	    WHERE status = 'active';
31	
32	-- Prevent multiple active rows for the same (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)
33	-- (covers the case where the same artifact is assigned to the same topic twice)
34	CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS artifact_topics_active_unique_key
35	    ON artifact_topics (artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)
36	    WHERE status = 'active';
37	
38	-- ============================================================
39	-- Indexes
40	-- ============================================================
41	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_topic_status
42	    ON artifact_topics (topic_id, status);
43	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_tagged_by
44	    ON artifact_topics (tagged_by_bot_id) WHERE tagged_by_bot_id IS NOT NULL;
45	
46	-- ============================================================
47	-- RLS: deny anon
48	-- ============================================================
49	ALTER TABLE artifact_topics ENABLE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
50	ALTER TABLE artifact_topics FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY;
```

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — artifact_topics JOIN cutover for reads

## Overview

Sprint 1 built the `artifact_topics` polymorphic join table and backfilled 235 active rows. S2a stamped insert sites and S2b retired dual-key paths + applied NOT NULL constraints. S3 cuts every artifact **read** over to the join: `JOIN artifact_topics at ON at.artifact_id = X.id AND at.artifact_table = '<T>' AND at.topic_id = <bind> AND at.status = 'active'`. Behavior is identical at launch — mediator's `primary_topic_id` is always `relationship` — but the read path becomes scope-aware, which is the foundation S4+ needs.

**Pivot from v1 plan (gate-driven).** Topic id is now a **defaulted** kwarg, not a required-raise one. This matches the brief's locked decision #4 ("every existing read site keyed off mediator behavior gets this topic_id **by default**") and means the off-limits test files (`test_hot_context.py`, etc.) continue to compile unchanged. The default resolves via `app.bots.registry.get_relationship_topic_id()`. The function still raises if the default itself is unavailable (startup race) — because then we genuinely cannot scope.

### Locked decisions (carried from brief; v1 questions Q1–Q4 are now settled)
1. **Helper** lives at `app/services/topic_filter.py::join_artifact_topics(table_alias, topic_id_param)`. No default for `topic_id_param` — caller must pass an explicit `$N`. Addresses gate flag `correctness-1`.
2. **Decay** is per-topic; module-level rationale comment required.
3. **OOB delivery-time guardrail** filters via `artifact_topics.topic_id = <topic_bind>`.
4. **Mediator primary topic = `relationship`**, defaulted into the function bodies via `get_relationship_topic_id()`.
5. **No multi-topic writes (S6); no scope enforcement (S4); no `scope` arg on read tools (S4).**
6. **Q1 settled: SHIP `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql`** creating the partial index `(topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active'` that the brief's EXPLAIN gate names. Existing `(topic_id, status)` non-partial index stays — they cover different read shapes.
7. **Q2 settled: scope `scoring.py::rescore_observations`** the same way as decay (housekeeping sibling).
8. **Q3 settled: write-tool linked-ID validations get the JOIN.** Specifically: `write_tools.py:889` (watch_items owner lookup), `:1246` (out_of_bounds owner lookup), and `_require_existing_distillation_links` (the existence checks for memories/observations/themes/distillations IDs at write time). UPDATEs in write_tools.py stay direct — S6 owns that — and the new lint regex is `FROM`-only by design (see Step 10 note).
9. **Q4 settled: `app/routers/admin.py` stays global** with a whitelist entry. Admin is operator-only.

### Constraints
- Dirty files **off-limits**: `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`, `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`. The default-kwarg pivot means none of these need edits.
- Dirty files where S3-scope edits are explicitly **allowed**: `app/services/hot_context.py`, `prompts.py`, `time_context.py` — query-rewrite only.
- Branch already exists: `s3-join-cutover` at HEAD `13fb67e`.
- S2b lesson: legacy `OR bot_id IS NULL` dual-key filters are gone. Pure equality.

### Caller enumeration (verified by grep)
- `build_hot_context`: `app/services/agentic.py` (has `ctx`), `app/staging.py:46` (no `ctx`; uses default), `tests/test_hot_context.py` × 8 at lines 255, 316, 338, 389, 428, 461, 491, 503 (off-limits; survive via default).
- `check_oob_with_policy`: `app/services/hooks.py:22`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:752-760`. (No `messaging.py` site — the v1 plan was wrong.)
- `summarize_partner_oob`: `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:763-765`, `tests/test_oob_countersummary.py:52, :65, :81`.
- `run_decay_housekeeping`: `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py` (the housekeeping handler), `tests/test_decay.py:93` (positional `now` kwarg only), `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:347` (monkeypatched with a `fake_decay` whose signature must absorb the new kwarg).
- `rescore_observations`: `app/services/decay.py:94` (only caller).

## Phase 1: Helper, plumbing, and preflight

### Step 1: Preflight + add read-path partial index (`scripts/`, `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** prod state: `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics WHERE status='active'` — expect ≥235. Use `DATABASE_URL` with `statement_cache_size=0` per S1 lesson #3.
2. **Backfill-completeness assertion** (closes gate flag `correctness-4`). Run:
   ```sql
   SELECT 'memories' AS t, count(*) FROM mediator.memories m
     WHERE NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM mediator.artifact_topics at
                       WHERE at.artifact_table='memories' AND at.artifact_id=m.id AND at.status='active')
   UNION ALL ...repeat for themes/observations/watch_items/distillations/out_of_bounds...
   ```
   Every row must be `0`. If any are non-zero, STOP — the cutover would silently drop those rows. (The property test in Step 8 cannot detect this drift on its own; this preflight is the gate.)
3. **Create** `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql` — adds the partial composite index the EXPLAIN gate names:
   ```sql
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_topic_artifact_active
       ON artifact_topics (topic_id, artifact_table)
       WHERE status = 'active';
   ```
   Apply via psql port 5432 session-mode (S2b lesson #8). The existing `(topic_id, status)` index stays — different shape, different read.

### Step 2: Add the topic-filter helper (`app/services/topic_filter.py`, `tests/test_topic_filter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `app/services/topic_filter.py`:
   ```python
   """SQL JOIN-fragment helper that scopes the six artifact tables by topic."""
   _ARTIFACT_TABLES = {
       "m": "memories", "t": "themes", "o": "observations",
       "w": "watch_items", "d": "distillations", "x": "out_of_bounds",
   }
   def join_artifact_topics(table_alias: str, topic_id_param: str) -> str:
       try:
           artifact_table = _ARTIFACT_TABLES[table_alias]
       except KeyError as exc:
           raise ValueError(f"unknown alias {table_alias!r}; known: {sorted(_ARTIFACT_TABLES)}") from exc
       return (
           f"JOIN artifact_topics _at_{table_alias} "
           f"ON _at_{table_alias}.artifact_table = '{artifact_table}' "
           f"AND _at_{table_alias}.artifact_id = {table_alias}.id "
           f"AND _at_{table_alias}.topic_id = {topic_id_param} "
           f"AND _at_{table_alias}.status = 'active'"
       )
   ```
   `topic_id_param` has **no default** — caller must pass the explicit `$N` (closes gate flag `correctness-1`).
2. **Add** `tests/test_topic_filter.py`: confirms fragment shape for all six aliases and that an unknown alias raises `ValueError`. Cheap, <1s.

### Step 3: Plumb a defaulted topic_id into the affected entry points (`app/services/hot_context.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, `app/services/oob_check.py`, `app/services/scoring.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`build_hot_context`** (`app/services/hot_context.py:173-180`): add keyword-only `primary_topic_id: UUID | None = None`. Inside the body, resolve `topic = primary_topic_id or get_relationship_topic_id()`. If `topic is None`, raise `RuntimeError("primary_topic_id unavailable: get_relationship_topic_id() not yet populated")` — startup-race guard, not a None-API guard. This pattern (default-then-raise) is what addresses gate flags `FLAG-001`, `issue_hints-1`, `all_locations-1`, `all_locations-2`, `callers-1`. Off-limits test files don't change.
2. **`run_decay_housekeeping`** (`app/services/decay.py:26`): same pattern — `topic_id: UUID | None = None` kwarg, resolve from `get_relationship_topic_id()` inside, raise if both are None. Addresses `FLAG-004`, `correctness-3`, `all_locations-5`, `callers-3`. Existing `tests/test_decay.py:93` call `run_decay_housekeeping(fake_pool, now=now)` keeps working. `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:347` `fake_decay` monkeypatch keeps working because it's accepting `**kwargs` shape (verify during execute; if it isn't, the new kwarg is keyword-only so existing positional patches don't break — verify pattern at runtime).
3. **`check_oob_with_policy`** (`app/services/oob_check.py:167-181`) and **`summarize_partner_oob`** (`:314-319`): same pattern — `topic_id: UUID | None = None`, resolve internally, raise if both None. Addresses `all_locations-3`, `all_locations-4`, `callers-2`, `callers-4`. `tests/test_oob_countersummary.py` × 3 call sites and `app/services/hooks.py:22` continue working via default.
4. **`rescore_observations`** (`app/services/scoring.py:125`): same pattern. Only caller is `decay.py:94`, which after Step 7 passes through the resolved topic id explicitly.
5. **Update explicit callers** to pass the value rather than relying on default (so the default is a safety net, not load-bearing in production paths):
   - `app/services/agentic.py` `build_hot_context(...)` call → add `primary_topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:752-760` `check_oob_with_policy(...)` → add `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:763-765` `summarize_partner_oob(...)` → add `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `app/services/hooks.py:22` `check_oob_with_policy(...)` → add `topic_id=` from the available context. Check `hooks.py` for what's in scope; if `ctx` is unavailable, pass `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()` explicitly.
   - `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py` decay-housekeeping invocation → add `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`.
   - `app/staging.py:46` → add `primary_topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()` explicitly so staging is unambiguous, not silently leaning on a runtime-cached default.

## Phase 2: Read-site rewrites

### Step 4: Rewrite `hot_context.py` reads (`app/services/hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Rewrite** the six SELECTs in the brief's required order. Each receives the resolved `topic_id` as the first bind. Per S3 pattern: prepend `topic_id` to `params`, then reference the JOIN at `$1`, and renumber any subsequent `$N` references in the same query. (Hot context queries are short, so this is cleaner than the append-last gymnastics — closes gate flag `correctness-1` for this file.)
   - `out_of_bounds` at `:227-235` — JOIN alias `x`, topic at `$1`, existing `[user.id, partner.id]` array becomes `$2`.
   - `memories` at `:247-258` — JOIN alias `m`, topic at `$1`. Keep `OR about_user_id IS NULL` (couple-level memories — content concern, orthogonal to scope).
   - `themes` at `:273-282` — JOIN alias `t`, topic at `$1`.
   - `watch_items` at `:292-300` — JOIN alias `w`, topic at `$1`, `user.id` becomes `$2`.
   - `observations` at `:315-327` — JOIN alias `o`, topic at `$1`.
   - `distillations` at `:344-360` — JOIN alias `d`, topic at `$1`, source-user array becomes `$2`.
2. **Use** the helper for every fragment; never inline.
3. **Preserve** all `ORDER BY`, `LIMIT`, `WHERE` filters (`status='active'`, `significance >= 3`, etc.).

### Step 5: Rewrite `read_tools.py` reads (`app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Strategy** for parameter binding (closes `correctness-1`): in each function, **prepend** `ctx.primary_topic_id` to `params` at the top of the builder, capture its position as `topic_idx = 1`, pass `f"${topic_idx}"` to the helper, and shift every subsequent `${len(params)}` reference by one. This is mechanical but explicit — no append-last surprise. Reviewer can grep for the prepend pattern across rewrites.
2. **Rewrite** each artifact-table SELECT with the helper:
   - `list_themes` at `:520-533` (alias `t`).
   - `get_theme` at `:537-547` (alias `t`).
   - `get_theme` related-memory at `:549-552` (alias `m`).
   - `get_theme` related-observation at `:553-556` (alias `o`).
   - `get_memories` at `:582-592` (alias `m`).
   - `list_watch_items` at `:609-620` (alias `w`).
   - `get_observations` at `:636-651` (alias `o`).
   - `get_distillations` at `:682-702` (alias `d`).
   - `get_oob` at `:739-749` (alias `x`).
3. **Keep** all secondary filters (sharing-default visibility, theme/memory ID filtering, etc.) untouched.

### Step 6: Rewrite `oob_check.py`; confirm `cross_thread_privacy.py` audit-clean (`app/services/oob_check.py`, `app/services/cross_thread_privacy.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`oob_check.py::_active_oob_entries`** at `:112-133` — add `topic_id: UUID` positional/kwarg, helper-JOIN the SELECT, alias `x`, topic at `$1`. Callers `check_oob_with_policy` and `summarize_partner_oob` forward the resolved topic from Step 3.
2. **`cross_thread_privacy.py`** — ripgrep confirms zero direct artifact-table reads (operates on messages and bridge_candidates). Record under "audit clean" in commit body; no SQL changes.

### Step 7: Rewrite `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `decay.py`, `scoring.py`; audit-clean `withheld_reviews.py` (`app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, `app/services/scoring.py`, `app/services/withheld_reviews.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`scheduled_job_handlers.py`**:
   - Weekly-summary counts at `:248-266` — rewrite each subquery to JOIN `artifact_topics` filtered by `topic_id = $2`; `$2` comes from `job.get('topic_id') or get_relationship_topic_id()` (mirrors the existing handler pattern at `:212`).
   - `_fetch_watch_item` at `:272-283` — add `topic_id` parameter, helper-JOIN.
2. **`decay.py`** — apply the per-topic policy comment block at the top of the module:
   ```
   # Decay is per-topic, not global.
   # A coach's career observations must not decay because a mediator
   # hasn't reinforced them. Each housekeeping run is scoped to a single
   # topic; the caller passes topic_id (defaulting to the relationship
   # topic at launch) and the UPDATEs join through artifact_topics to
   # restrict the row set.
   ```
   Then rewrite the five UPDATEs at `:39-93`:
   ```sql
   UPDATE themes
   SET status = 'dormant', updated_at = $1
   FROM artifact_topics at
   WHERE at.artifact_table = 'themes'
     AND at.artifact_id = themes.id
     AND at.topic_id = $2
     AND at.status = 'active'
     AND themes.status = 'active'
     AND COALESCE(themes.last_reinforced_at, themes.first_seen_at) <= $1::timestamptz - interval '6 weeks'
   ```
   Same shape for the other UPDATE on `themes`, both `observations` UPDATEs, and the `watch_items` UPDATE. The helper is for SELECT shape — these are UPDATE … FROM, so inline the JOIN (and note this in Step 10's lint design).
   - Forward `topic_id` to `rescore_observations` at `:94`.
3. **`scoring.py::rescore_observations`** at `:125-165` — add `topic_id: UUID | None = None` kwarg (default-then-raise pattern from Step 3). Helper-JOIN the SELECT at `:132-142` (alias `o`, topic at `$2`, existing `prompt_version_threshold` slides to `$1` — or simpler, prepend topic; the `UPDATE` at `:148-160` is keyed by `id` so no scope change is needed there once the SELECT has already filtered to the right topic. Confirm during execute.)
4. **`withheld_reviews.py`** — ripgrep confirms only INSERTs into `withheld_outbound_reviews`. Record under "audit clean".

### Step 8: Property/frozen-fixture test (`tests/test_hot_context_join_cutover.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** new file (off-limits rule on `test_hot_context.py`).
2. **Fixture**: fake pool seeded with one each of memory, theme, observation, watch_item, distillation, oob — plus an `artifact_topics` row for **every** seeded artifact linking it to the relationship topic. (This is the contract S2a enforces in prod; the test mirrors it.)
3. **Strategy**: render hot context, compare to a frozen expected-output constant captured pre-cutover from the S2b tip in a scratch worktree, embedded as `_S2B_RENDERED_HOT_CONTEXT = """..."""`.
4. **Drift safeguard** (closes gate flag `correctness-4`): also assert that the **count** of items in each list (`memories`, `themes`, `observations`, `watch_items`, `distillations`, `active_oob`, `distillations`) equals the seeded count. If a backfill gap silently drops a row post-JOIN, the count diverges from the seed and the test fails, even when the rendered text happens to match for unrelated reasons. (The prod-side gap is caught by Step 1's backfill-completeness preflight; this is the test-side mirror.)
5. **Required-kwarg side gate is dropped.** Because `primary_topic_id` now defaults rather than raises, we don't add a test asserting `ValueError`. Instead, add `test_build_hot_context_raises_when_topic_registry_unset`: monkeypatch `get_relationship_topic_id` to return `None`, call without kwarg, assert `RuntimeError`. Locks in the startup-race guard.

### Step 9: EXPLAIN regression script (`scripts/explain_artifact_topics_reads.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the script. `--help` parses (S1 lesson #1). Open asyncpg with `statement_cache_size=0`. Schema `mediator` (S1 lesson #5).
2. **Six representative queries** (one per artifact table) mirroring hot_context shapes.
3. **Assert** plan uses `Index Scan` / `Index Only Scan` / `Bitmap Index Scan` on `idx_artifact_topics_topic_artifact_active` (the new partial index from Step 1). Fallback to `idx_artifact_topics_topic_status` allowed but flagged as `WARN`. `Seq Scan` on `artifact_topics` → `FAIL`. Non-zero exit on any FAIL.

### Step 10: Lint script for read-side direct artifact accesses (`scripts/lint_artifact_reads.py`, `tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Create** `scripts/lint_artifact_reads.py`. Scans `app/` for regex `\bFROM\s+(memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)\b`. For each hit, extracts the enclosing triple-quoted SQL (same approach as `lint_inserts.py`), then checks that the string also contains `JOIN artifact_topics` (helper-emitted alias prefix `_at_` is the signal). If not → violation.
2. **Whitelist** (Q4 settled): `app/services/topic_filter.py`, `tests/`, `migrations/`, the lint script itself, `app/routers/admin.py` (operator view, intentionally global — add a one-line `# allowlisted: admin operator view` comment at each admin SQL site so the reason is in the code).
3. **Scope explicit, by-design**: regex is `FROM`-only. UPDATEs in `write_tools.py` are intentionally not caught — S6 owns them. Document this in the script's docstring so reviewers understand the omission (closes gate flag `scope-3`). Add a TODO comment naming S6.
4. **Wire** into pytest as `tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py::test_lint_artifact_reads_clean` — subprocess call, assert exit 0, mirrors existing patterns. CLI supports `--help` and `--dir`.

### Step 11: Decay per-topic test (`tests/test_decay.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `tests/test_decay.py` is **not** in the off-limits list. The existing positional-kwarg call at `:93` (`run_decay_housekeeping(fake_pool, now=now)`) keeps working unchanged because `topic_id` is now defaulted (Step 3).
2. **Add** `test_decay_only_touches_rows_for_current_topic`: seed two themes / two observations / two watch_items, one each in `relationship` topic, one each in a synthetic second topic id. Stub `get_relationship_topic_id` to return the relationship topic id, then call `run_decay_housekeeping(pool, now=...)` (default path) — assert only the relationship-scoped rows mutated.
3. **Add** `test_decay_scopes_when_explicit_topic_passed`: call `run_decay_housekeeping(pool, topic_id=other_topic_id, now=...)`, assert only the other-topic rows mutated.

### Step 12: Write-tool linked-ID validation rewrites (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Scope (Q3 settled)**: write-tool linked-ID *validations* (SELECTs that verify referenced artifacts exist) are read sites and get the JOIN. UPDATEs stay direct — S6 problem.
2. **Sites**:
   - `:889` `SELECT owner_user_id FROM watch_items WHERE id=$1` → helper-JOIN on `w`, topic at `$1`, id at `$2`. Source topic from `ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `:1119` SELECT inside `WITH old AS (... FROM distillations WHERE id=$1 AND status='active')` in `revise_distillation` → helper-JOIN on `d`. This is more involved because it's inside a CTE; alternative is to leave the inner WHERE as-is and add a sibling existence check that gates on `artifact_topics`. **Decision**: add a sibling existence check immediately before the CTE — keep the CTE shape stable; if the topic-scoped check returns 0, raise `ToolCallRejected` before the CTE runs. This is the minimum-diff option and matches the existing rejection flow.
   - `:1246` `SELECT owner_id FROM out_of_bounds WHERE id=$1` → helper-JOIN on `x`, topic at `$1`, id at `$2`.
   - `_require_existing_distillation_links` near `:1100` — find the function body, identify its SELECTs against `memories`, `observations`, `themes`, `distillations`, helper-JOIN each. Source topic from `ctx.primary_topic_id`.
3. **Tests**: existing write-tool tests use mock pools (search `test_tools.py` — off-limits — so do **not** edit; instead, exercise via `tests/test_agentic.py` and any non-dirty test files that import write tools, plus a new targeted test if needed). Add at most one new test file `tests/test_write_tools_topic_scoping.py` only if no existing non-dirty test covers the validation path.

## Phase 3: Validation

### Step 13: Run the targeted test suite
**Scope:** Small
1. **Cheap unit tests first**:
   ```
   pytest tests/test_topic_filter.py
   pytest tests/test_decay.py
   pytest tests/test_hot_context_join_cutover.py
   pytest tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py
   pytest tests/test_oob_countersummary.py
   pytest tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py
   pytest tests/test_hot_context.py    # off-limits to EDIT, but MUST keep passing
   ```
2. **Broader**: `pytest tests/ -x`. Stop on first failure per brief stop-condition #1.
3. **Both lint scripts** as bare CLIs: `--help` parse check, then run on `app/`, confirm zero violations.

### Step 14: EXPLAIN check against prod
**Scope:** Small
1. `python scripts/explain_artifact_topics_reads.py --help` — parse check.
2. Run against prod `DATABASE_URL`. All six queries → PASS (Index Scan on the new partial index, or WARN if falling back to the older index — STOP only on `Seq Scan` per brief stop-condition #3).

### Step 15: Manual snapshot (info-only)
**Scope:** Small
1. Eval-baseline re-run remains skipped per the brief (Supabase pooler issue, same posture as S2a). Capture an informal hot-context render against a staging fixture only if time permits.

## Execution Order
1. Preflight + new index (Step 1). Helper + plumbing (Steps 2, 3).
2. Read rewrites in brief order: `hot_context.py` (4), `read_tools.py` (5), `oob_check.py` (6), `scheduled_job_handlers.py` + `decay.py` + `scoring.py` (7).
3. Property/frozen-fixture test (8). EXPLAIN script (9). Lint + wire-up (10). Decay test (11).
4. Write-tool linked-ID rewrites (12).
5. Validation (Steps 13–15).

## Validation Order
1. **Backfill-completeness preflight** on prod (Step 1.2) — gates the entire sprint.
2. **Unit-level**: topic-filter helper, decay-scope, hot_context_join_cutover, lint test.
3. **Integration**: full `pytest tests/ -x` — every previously-passing test still passes (especially the off-limits `tests/test_hot_context.py` and `tests/test_tools.py`).
4. **Lint gates**: `lint_inserts.py` exit 0 (no regression), `lint_artifact_reads.py` exit 0.
5. **Property test**: hot-context frozen-fixture + count-equality drift safeguard.
6. **EXPLAIN gate** against prod.

  "assumptions": [
    "Topic id is a defaulted kwarg on `build_hot_context`, `run_decay_housekeeping`, `check_oob_with_policy`, `summarize_partner_oob`, and `rescore_observations`. The default resolves via `app.bots.registry.get_relationship_topic_id()`. A `RuntimeError` is raised only when the registry itself returns None (startup race). This honors the brief's 'by default' wording in locked decision #4 and lets off-limits test files compile unchanged.",
    "`migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql` ships in this sprint creating the partial index `(topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active'` that the EXPLAIN gate names. The existing `(topic_id, status)` index stays \u2014 it covers a different read shape and removing it is out of scope.",
    "`app/services/scoring.py::rescore_observations` is treated as a decay sibling and gets the same defaulted-topic kwarg + JOIN on its SELECT. The UPDATE is keyed by id post-filter, so no scope change is required at the UPDATE.",
    "Write-tool linked-ID validations (`write_tools.py:889`, `:1246`, the CTE at `:1119`, `_require_existing_distillation_links`) are read sites and get the JOIN in Step 12. The CTE at `:1119` gets a sibling pre-check rather than restructuring the CTE itself (minimum-diff option).",
    "Direct UPDATEs in `write_tools.py` (memories, themes, observations, watch_items, distillations, out_of_bounds) stay unchanged \u2014 S6 owns multi-topic writes. The read-side lint is FROM-only by design; the script docstring records this scope omission and adds a TODO naming S6.",
    "`app/routers/admin.py` stays global; whitelisted in the lint with a one-line `# allowlisted: admin operator view` comment at each of the six SQL sites so the reason is documented inline.",
    "The helper `join_artifact_topics(table_alias, topic_id_param)` has no default for `topic_id_param`. Caller must pass an explicit `$N` string. This prevents silent positional-bind mistakes.",
    "For `hot_context.py` and `read_tools.py` rewrites, `topic_id` is **prepended** to the params list and bound at `$1`; all downstream `${len(params)}` references shift by one. The pattern is mechanical and grep-able, addressing the v1 plan's ambiguity around append-vs-prepend.",
    "`app/staging.py:46` explicitly passes `primary_topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`. Staging is not a runtime hot path so leaning on the default would be silently load-bearing; explicit is better.",
    "`hooks.py:22` explicitly passes `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()` because no `ctx` is in scope at that call site (verify during execute; if a `ctx` is available, prefer `ctx.primary_topic_id`).",
    "Step 1.2 backfill-completeness preflight is a hard gate: any non-zero count for missing artifact_topics rows STOPs the sprint before any code change lands. This complements the test-side count-equality safeguard in Step 8.4.",
    "Property test snapshot is captured against the S2b tip (`13fb67e`) in a scratch worktree, embedded as a dedented string constant. The test also compares list lengths to seeded counts so a silently-dropped row trips the assertion even if the rendered text accidentally still matches.",
    "All asyncpg one-off scripts use `statement_cache_size=0` against `DATABASE_URL` (Supabase port 6543 transaction-mode); DDL migrations use port 5432 session-mode."
  ],

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the 0020 migration plan for user_identities omits the verified_at timestamptz column present in architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl, while claiming 'exactly per \u00a73/\u00a712 ddl'. since account-linking workflows are deferred beyond s1, the column is not functionally load-bearing yet, but the claim of exact ddl match is incorrect.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-009",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the resolve_binding sql in app/services/routing.py uses limit 1 without order by. if a user ever has both a solo binding and a dyad binding to the same bot, the returned row is non-deterministic. the schema has no unique constraint preventing multiple bindings per (bot_id, user_id). since s1 only seeds one dyad binding and the module is not wired into inbound yet, this is latent future risk rather than an immediate s1 blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]

  "does-the-change-touch-all-locations-and-supporting-infrastructure": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-008",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: routing unit tests require extending fakepool with `channels`, `user_identities`, `bot_bindings`, `dyads`, and `dyad_members` dicts so the sql patterns in `resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, and `resolve_binding` can match against stored rows. however, `tests/conftest.py` is explicitly listed in the brief as a dirty file that must not be touched. the revised plan says \"extend fakepool minimally\" twice (step 9 and step 11 assumption) but never specifies how to do so without modifying `conftest.py`\u2014for example by defining a local `routingfakepool(fakepool)` subclass inside `tests/test_routing.py` or by monkeypatching the class in that test module. if the executor follows the plan literally and edits `conftest.py`, they will violate a locked constraint. if they do not extend fakepool, the routing unit tests cannot be implemented as written. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: step 3 updates scripts/seed_channels.py to import _decode_discord_user_id from the new shared module. the seeder also previously had a regex `_discord_bot_user_id_re` constant (or similar) inline; if the seeder's local regex remains while the new module redefines it, the two could drift. plan should explicitly say the seeder deletes its local copy.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: plan step 14.3 lists hooks.py emitters but `paused_for_user` does not currently call `logger.*` (verified by reading hooks.py). the plan instructs to log `extra={user_id, bot_id}` \u2014 if there is no existing log call, the executor must add one, which is a behavior change rather than an annotation. minor scope creep.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-023",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py is still listed as a deliverable (step 2.1) and a smoke test (step 2.7 `--help` invocation). if t2 is deferred, the script could either (a) not be written, or (b) be written but its run deferred. plan should specify which. the smoke test alone (`--help`) does not require the pooler.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-024",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 0 contains both step 1 (parity + identity seeding pre-flight) and step 2 (eval baseline). step 1 is independent of the pooler block \u2014 it queries via `psql` or any working connection \u2014 and should not be deferred. plan should make clear step 1 still gates phase 1, but step 2 is parked.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: step 13.2 adds `bot_id` kwarg to hooks.paused_for_user. callers grep shows it is called from various places; the plan only enumerates messaging.py:170,335 as new bot_id-passing sites. other callers continue to call without bot_id (none default), which is fine, but plan should call out that all existing callers continue to work without modification.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-025",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: evals.runner.run_scenarios is referenced by the deferred step 2.1; plan doesn't enumerate that runner's callers or its schema dependencies. since step 2 is being deferred, this is moot \u2014 but if the script is still authored as a step 2.1 deliverable, it must not import anything that hits the pooler at import time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: plan step 18 strips `bot_spec` defaults from `tests/_scope_helpers.py:make_mediator_ctx`, which has six call sites across `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` (5 calls) and `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` (1 call) that rely on the implicit default. the executor must update all six call sites to pass `bot_spec` explicitly. however, plan step 20's stop-condition nuance lists only steps 14\u201317 as the 'intentional-update list' and omits step 18. if the executor misses a call site and those tests fail, the stop condition text would classify the failure as 'unexpected' and trigger stop, even though the root cause is an incomplete step 18 execution. the stop condition should explicitly include step 18 (or enumerate `test_s2a_stamping.py` and `test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py`) so the executor has permission to fix residual failures in those files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "issue-hints-completeness": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-027",
      "concern": "issue_hints / completeness: user note explicitly defers t2 (eval baseline capture) due to supabase transaction-mode pooler not preserving set search_path. plan still has step 2 in phase 0 as a gating task, with phase 0 listed first in execution order and the final validation order step diffing against the baseline. plan body must be updated to mark step 2 as deferred, downgrade the related success criterion from 'must' to 'should/deferred', and remove the post-stamp diff from the must-do validation list.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-005",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the architecture doc \u00a712.2 ddl for `user_identities` includes a `verified_at timestamptz` column. the plan's 0020 migration ddl omits this column entirely. while account-linking is deferred to s4+, omitting the column now means a future `alter table user_identities add column verified_at` is required, creating unnecessary schema churn. the column can safely be created nullable with no data in s1, matching the architecture doc verbatim.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-006",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the architecture doc \u00a712.1 ddl for `channels` includes `config jsonb not null default '{}'::jsonb`. the plan's 0020 migration ddl omits this column. like `verified_at`, this is a small additive column that should be created now to match the locked schema; deferring it requires a later migration. the executor should add the column to the 0020 ddl.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 10 claims `populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` reads 'db-populated fields' for `primary_topic_slug`, `participants_shape`, `read_scopes`, etc. however, the `bots` table ddl in the plan only defines `id`, `display_name`, and `created_at` \u2014 none of the botspec scope fields have db columns. in practice the function will hardcode mediator defaults (`primary_topic_slug='relationship'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`) and only read `display_name` from the db row. the plan's wording could mislead the executor into writing a `select * from bots` expecting columns that do not exist. the implementation should explicitly select only `display_name` and hardcode the rest, or the ddl should be expanded to store bot config. the architecture doc does not specify these columns on `bots`, so hardcoding is correct for s1, but the plan text should be clarified.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the locked brief \u00a716.1 work item 1 explicitly requires 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' seeded inside migration 0020. the revised plan instead moves channel seeding to a runtime script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. while the rationale (env vars differ across environments) is pragmatic, this is a concrete divergence from the locked architecture-doc brief which the plan must consume, not override. if the executor applies only the sql migrations, the `channels` table will be empty and routing will have no rows to resolve, breaking the mid-sprint checkpoint 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' gate. the runtime script must be documented as a mandatory post-migration step, but the brief expected it in the migration itself.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-012",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: debt-005/006/007/010: verified_at and channels.config jsonb added to 0020; step 10 clarifies populate_mediator_spec_from_db reads only display_name; channel seeding documented as mandatory post-migration script. debt-010 remains a literal divergence from \u00a716.1 'channels seeded in migration 0020' wording but is now explicitly explained.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-014",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 13.2 adds optional bot_id kwarg to hooks.paused_for_user but the function's body still calls `system_state.is_paused(_pool)` only (global). the added parameter is wired in but not consumed in s2a \u2014 essentially a dead parameter until s2b. plan does not explicitly say so; executor may either leave it unused or call user_bot_paused. worth flagging for clarity.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-028",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: user note's deferral applies only to t2 (eval baseline). the core s2a deliverable \u2014 stamping insert sites, rekeying coalescer/suppression, observability, per-bot pause \u2014 is independent of t2 and proceeds. plan does not currently distinguish; deferring t2 requires explicit text saying 'phase 0 step 2 is deferred to s3 pre-flight or end-of-sprint manual capture'.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-029",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: eval-baseline-related criteria in the success_criteria list (criterion 13: 'eval baseline captured by writing scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py ...') will be unmeetable if t2 is deferred. plan should downgrade that criterion's priority from 'must' to 'should' or mark deferred.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-032",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 18 instructs the executor to grep for bot_spec= to enumerate fixture defaults, but this pattern misses the actual signature default bot_spec: botspec | none = none in tests/_scope_helpers.py:35. there are 7 call sites across tests/test_s2a_stamping.py (6 calls) and tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py (1 call) that invoke make_mediator_ctx() without passing bot_spec and rely on that default. because the grep won't surface the signature default, the executor may miss stripping it and updating the call sites, leaving the 'should' criterion partially unmet.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — artifact_topics JOIN cutover for reads

## Overview

S3 cuts every artifact **read** in `memories | themes | observations | watch_items | distillations | out_of_bounds` over to a `JOIN artifact_topics` filtered by `topic_id = <bind> AND status = 'active'`. Behavior is identical at launch (mediator's topic is always `relationship`); the read path becomes scope-aware so S4+ can layer multi-bot scoping on top.

**Root-cause framing of v2 critique (kept honest).** The v2 plan introduced a defaulted `primary_topic_id` kwarg resolving via `get_relationship_topic_id()`. That direction is correct, but it failed to acknowledge that:

1. The registry cache `_RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID` (`app/bots/registry.py:14`) is `None` until `populate_topic_ids_from_db` runs at startup — and tests don't run startup. So in tests, the default resolves to `None` and the "raise if both None" branch fires.
2. `tests/conftest.py` `FakePool.fetch` uses string-pattern matching (`compact.startswith(...)` at conftest.py:1403/1406/1409, `'FROM memories' in compact` at :1501, similar at :1217/1369/1371/1480/1517/1533/1560). A new `JOIN artifact_topics` clause is invisible to those matchers; **substring** matchers (`'FROM memories' in compact`) still match because the artifact-table name is preserved, but the three `startswith` matchers fail.
3. `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:343` defines `async def fake_decay(pool)` — positional-only. The plan needs to choose: don't pass `topic_id=` at the call site, OR widen `fake_decay`. Either works; the clearer fix is to widen the fake.
4. Step 10's lint regex `\bFROM\s+(memories|themes|...)` does **not** match `FROM artifact_topics` (artifact_topics isn't in the alternation). Decay's `UPDATE themes ... FROM artifact_topics at ...` is therefore NOT flagged by the lint, and no UPDATE-shape whitelist is needed. The v2 critique's `correctness-3` premise misreads the regex; the plan still clarifies this explicitly so reviewers don't re-raise the concern.

v3 lands three targeted plan additions to ground v2's pivot in the test infrastructure, plus the clarification on (4):

- **Step 4 (NEW)**: `tests/conftest.py` registry-bootstrap autouse fixture and FakePool matcher updates.
- **Step 11 (UPDATED)**: widen `fake_decay` at `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:343` to absorb `**kwargs`.
- **Step 10 (CLARIFIED)**: explicit note that decay's `UPDATE … FROM artifact_topics` does not trigger the FROM-only regex; no whitelist required.

### Locked decisions (carried forward; no relitigation)
1. Helper at `app/services/topic_filter.py::join_artifact_topics(table_alias, topic_id_param)`. `topic_id_param` has **no default** — caller passes explicit `$N`.
2. Decay is per-topic; module-level rationale comment required.
3. OOB delivery-time guardrail filters via `artifact_topics.topic_id = <bind>`.
4. Mediator primary topic = `relationship`, defaulted into function bodies via `get_relationship_topic_id()`.
5. No multi-topic writes (S6); no scope enforcement beyond hardcoded primary (S4); no `scope` arg on read tools (S4).
6. Migration `0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql` ships, adding the partial index `(topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active'` the EXPLAIN gate names. Existing `(topic_id, status)` non-partial index stays.
7. `scoring.py::rescore_observations` scoped same as decay.
8. Write-tool linked-ID *validations* (read sites) get the JOIN: `:889`, the CTE at `:1119`, `:1246`, `_require_existing_distillation_links`. UPDATEs in `write_tools.py` stay direct (S6 owns them).
9. `app/routers/admin.py` stays global; whitelisted in lint with `# allowlisted: admin operator view` at each SQL site.

### Constraints
- Off-limits: `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`, `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`.
- `tests/conftest.py` and `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py` are **NOT** off-limits — edits are free for the test-infrastructure work in Steps 4 and 11.
- Allowed dirty files (query-rewrite scope only): `app/services/hot_context.py`, `prompts.py`, `time_context.py`.
- Branch `s3-join-cutover` at HEAD `13fb67e`.

## Phase 1: Helper, plumbing, test infra, preflight

### Step 1: Preflight + read-path partial index (`scripts/`, `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** prod state: `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics WHERE status='active'` (≥235). asyncpg with `statement_cache_size=0`.

Each result MUST be 0. STOP otherwise — the cutover would silently drop those rows.

   ```sql
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_topic_artifact_active
       ON artifact_topics (topic_id, artifact_table)
       WHERE status = 'active';
   ```

**Scope:** Medium

### Step 4: Test-infra: registry bootstrap + FakePool matcher updates (`tests/conftest.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (closes gate flags `FLAG-008`, `FLAG-009`, `correctness-1`, `all_locations-1`, `all_locations-2`, `callers-2`, `callers-3`, `callers-4`, `issue_hints`, `FLAG-001`, `FLAG-004`).

1. **Registry bootstrap** — add a session-scoped autouse fixture near the top of `tests/conftest.py`:
   ```python
   _TEST_RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = UUID("00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001")

   @pytest.fixture(autouse=True, scope="session")
   def _seed_relationship_topic_id():
       import app.bots.registry as _reg
       _reg._RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID = _TEST_RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID
       yield
   ```
   This makes `get_relationship_topic_id()` return a stable test UUID without running app startup. Every off-limits test call site (`test_hot_context.py` × 8, `test_decay.py:93`, `test_oob_countersummary.py` × 3) now resolves the default and the `RuntimeError` branch never fires. Use a non-nil UUID with version-4 layout so any code asserting `uuid.version == 4` still passes.
2. **FakePool matcher updates**. Audit the three `startswith` matchers at `conftest.py:1403, 1406, 1409`:
   ```python
   # before
   if compact.startswith("SELECT id FROM themes WHERE id = ANY"):
   # after
   if "FROM themes" in compact and "WHERE id = ANY" in compact:
   ```
   The same shape applies at `:1406` (`memories`) and `:1409` (`observations`). The substring matchers at `:1217, :1369, :1371, :1480, :1501, :1517, :1533, :1560` already use `'FROM <table>' in compact` — those survive the JOIN cutover unchanged because the artifact-table name is preserved post-rewrite (the JOIN is appended, not substituted). Verify each during execute; if any matcher narrows further (e.g., asserts specific clause shape), relax it to substring.
3. **artifact_topics handling in FakePool**. FakePool currently has no `FROM artifact_topics` matcher. Rewritten queries reference it only inside the JOIN clause; FakePool returns rows for the artifact table and never sees the JOIN as a top-level FROM. So no new matcher is needed for the JOIN itself. Add ONE explicit comment in conftest.py near the artifact-table matchers stating this so future readers understand why the JOIN is invisible to the fake.
4. **Defensive sanity test** — add `tests/test_conftest_registry_bootstrap.py` (one test): call `get_relationship_topic_id()` and assert it returns the test UUID. Locks the fixture in.
