**Scope:** Small
1. `_active_oob_entries` at `:112-133` — add `topic_id: UUID` param, helper-JOIN the SELECT (alias `x`, topic at `$1`, existing `owner_ids` array slides to `$2`). `check_oob_with_policy` and `summarize_partner_oob` forward the resolved topic.
2. `cross_thread_privacy.py` — no direct artifact reads (confirmed by grep). Record as "audit clean" in commit body.

### Step 8: Rewrite `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `decay.py`, `scoring.py`; audit-clean `withheld_reviews.py`
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`scheduled_job_handlers.py`**: weekly-summary subqueries at `:248-266` — helper-JOIN each, topic id from `job.get('topic_id') or get_relationship_topic_id()`. `_fetch_watch_item` at `:272-283` — add `topic_id` param, helper-JOIN.
2. **`decay.py`**: add the top-of-module policy comment. Rewrite five UPDATEs using `UPDATE <table> ... FROM artifact_topics at WHERE at.artifact_table='<table>' AND at.artifact_id = <table>.id AND at.topic_id = $2 AND at.status = 'active' AND <table>.status = '...' AND ...`. These are NOT helper-emitted (helper is SELECT shape only) — inline the JOIN. **Note for Step 10**: the FROM-only lint regex does not match `FROM artifact_topics`, so the decay UPDATEs are not lint violations; no whitelist needed.
3. **`scoring.py::rescore_observations`** at `:125-165` — add `topic_id: UUID | None = None` kwarg with the standard default-resolution pattern. Helper-JOIN the SELECT at `:132-142`. UPDATE at `:148-160` is keyed by id post-filter; no scope change needed there.
4. **`withheld_reviews.py`** — INSERTs only into `withheld_outbound_reviews`. Audit clean.

## Phase 3: Tests, lint, validation

### Step 9: Property/frozen-fixture test (`tests/test_hot_context_join_cutover.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. New file (off-limits rule on `test_hot_context.py`).
2. Fixture seeds one each of the six artifact families plus matching `artifact_topics` rows linking each to the relationship topic id (the same UUID seeded by Step 4's fixture).
3. Snapshot strategy: capture rendered hot context from S2b tip (`13fb67e`) in a scratch worktree, embed as `_S2B_RENDERED_HOT_CONTEXT = """..."""`.
4. **Drift safeguard**: also assert list lengths match seeded counts. If a backfill gap causes post-JOIN rows to silently drop, the count diverges and the test fails.
5. Add `test_build_hot_context_raises_when_topic_registry_unset` — monkeypatch `get_relationship_topic_id` to return `None`, call without `primary_topic_id`, assert `RuntimeError`. Locks in the startup-race guard.

### Step 10: EXPLAIN regression script (`scripts/explain_artifact_topics_reads.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `--help` parses. asyncpg with `statement_cache_size=0`; schema `mediator`.
2. Six representative queries (one per artifact table) mirror hot_context shapes.
3. Assert plan uses `Index Scan` / `Index Only Scan` / `Bitmap Index Scan` on `idx_artifact_topics_topic_artifact_active` (PASS), fallback to `idx_artifact_topics_topic_status` (WARN), `Seq Scan` on `artifact_topics` (FAIL). Non-zero exit on any FAIL.

### Step 11: Read-side lint + fake_decay widening (`scripts/lint_artifact_reads.py`, `tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`)
**Scope:** Small (closes gate flags `correctness-2`, `correctness-3`, `callers-1`, `FLAG-010`)

1. **Lint** — `scripts/lint_artifact_reads.py` scans `app/` for `\bFROM\s+(memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)\b`. For each hit, extract enclosing triple-quoted SQL (same as `lint_inserts.py`), check that the SQL also contains `JOIN artifact_topics`. Whitelist: `app/services/topic_filter.py`, `tests/`, `migrations/`, the lint itself, `app/routers/admin.py`. Docstring records: regex is `FROM`-only; UPDATE-form direct accesses in `write_tools.py` are intentionally deferred to S6; `UPDATE … FROM artifact_topics …` in `decay.py` is NOT matched because `artifact_topics` isn't in the alternation (closes the v2 `correctness-3` mis-read explicitly).
2. **CLI** — `--help`, `--dir` flags.
3. **Wire** — `tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py::test_lint_artifact_reads_clean` runs the lint via subprocess, asserts exit 0.
4. **Add admin.py allowlist comments** — at `app/routers/admin.py:177, :182, :192, :202, :216, :268` add `# allowlisted: admin operator view` so the lint's whitelist has an inline justification.
5. **fake_decay widening** — edit `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:343` from `async def fake_decay(pool):` to `async def fake_decay(pool, **kwargs):`. Belt-and-braces with Step 3.2's "don't pass `topic_id=` at the call site" decision: even if a future change re-adds the kwarg, the fake absorbs it. Minimal, future-proof.

### Step 12: Decay per-topic tests (`tests/test_decay.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `tests/test_decay.py` not off-limits. Pre-existing `run_decay_housekeeping(fake_pool, now=now)` at `:93` now survives via Step 4 registry bootstrap.
2. Add `test_decay_only_touches_rows_for_current_topic` and `test_decay_scopes_when_explicit_topic_passed`.

### Step 13: Write-tool linked-ID validation rewrites (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `:889` `SELECT owner_user_id FROM watch_items WHERE id=$1` → helper-JOIN on `w`, topic at `$1`, id at `$2`.
2. `:1119` CTE in `revise_distillation` — add a sibling existence pre-check (topic-scoped) before the CTE; raise `ToolCallRejected` if it returns 0. Keeps the CTE shape stable.
3. `:1246` `SELECT owner_id FROM out_of_bounds WHERE id=$1` → helper-JOIN on `x`, topic at `$1`, id at `$2`.
4. `_require_existing_distillation_links` near `:1100` — helper-JOIN each existence SELECT against `memories`, `observations`, `themes`, `distillations`.
5. Only add a new test if existing non-off-limits coverage misses the path; `tests/test_tools.py` is off-limits, so prefer covering via `tests/test_agentic.py` or a new `tests/test_write_tools_topic_scoping.py`.

## Phase 4: Validation

### Step 14: Run the targeted test suite
**Scope:** Small
1. Cheap unit tests first: `test_topic_filter`, `test_conftest_registry_bootstrap`, `test_decay`, `test_hot_context_join_cutover`, `test_lint_artifact_reads`, `test_oob_countersummary`, `test_scheduled_jobs`.
2. Broader: `pytest tests/ -x`. Off-limits files (`test_hot_context.py`, `test_tools.py`) MUST keep passing untouched.
3. Lint scripts: `--help` parse + run on `app/`, expect exit 0 for both.

### Step 15: EXPLAIN check + manual snapshot
**Scope:** Small
1. `python scripts/explain_artifact_topics_reads.py --help`; run against prod; expect PASS on all six (WARN on older index acceptable; STOP on Seq Scan).
2. Eval baseline skipped per brief; optional informal hot-context render against staging.

## Execution Order
1. Preflight + new index (Step 1). Helper (Step 2). Defaulted-kwarg plumbing (Step 3). Test infra (Step 4).
2. Read rewrites: `hot_context.py` (5), `read_tools.py` (6), `oob_check.py` (7), `scheduled_job_handlers.py` + `decay.py` + `scoring.py` (8).
3. Property test (9), EXPLAIN script (10), lint + fake_decay widening + admin comments (11), decay tests (12), write-tool validations (13).
4. Validation (14, 15).

## Validation Order
1. Backfill-completeness preflight gate (Step 1.2) — STOPs the sprint if non-zero.
2. Unit tests (topic-filter helper, registry bootstrap, decay scope, hot_context_join_cutover, lint test).
3. Full `pytest tests/ -x`. Off-limits tests must keep passing.
4. Both lint scripts exit zero.
5. Property test (frozen-fixture + count-equality safeguard).
6. EXPLAIN gate against prod.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: step 3 updates scripts/seed_channels.py to import _decode_discord_user_id from the new shared module. the seeder also previously had a regex `_discord_bot_user_id_re` constant (or similar) inline; if the seeder's local regex remains while the new module redefines it, the two could drift. plan should explicitly say the seeder deletes its local copy.",

      ]
    },
    {

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: plan step 14.3 lists hooks.py emitters but `paused_for_user` does not currently call `logger.*` (verified by reading hooks.py). the plan instructs to log `extra={user_id, bot_id}` \u2014 if there is no existing log call, the executor must add one, which is a behavior change rather than an annotation. minor scope creep.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-023",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py is still listed as a deliverable (step 2.1) and a smoke test (step 2.7 `--help` invocation). if t2 is deferred, the script could either (a) not be written, or (b) be written but its run deferred. plan should specify which. the smoke test alone (`--help`) does not require the pooler.",

    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-024",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 0 contains both step 1 (parity + identity seeding pre-flight) and step 2 (eval baseline). step 1 is independent of the pooler block \u2014 it queries via `psql` or any working connection \u2014 and should not be deferred. plan should make clear step 1 still gates phase 1, but step 2 is parked.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: step 13.2 adds `bot_id` kwarg to hooks.paused_for_user. callers grep shows it is called from various places; the plan only enumerates messaging.py:170,335 as new bot_id-passing sites. other callers continue to call without bot_id (none default), which is fine, but plan should call out that all existing callers continue to work without modification.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-025",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: evals.runner.run_scenarios is referenced by the deferred step 2.1; plan doesn't enumerate that runner's callers or its schema dependencies. since step 2 is being deferred, this is moot \u2014 but if the script is still authored as a step 2.1 deliverable, it must not import anything that hits the pooler at import time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: plan step 18 strips `bot_spec` defaults from `tests/_scope_helpers.py:make_mediator_ctx`, which has six call sites across `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` (5 calls) and `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` (1 call) that rely on the implicit default. the executor must update all six call sites to pass `bot_spec` explicitly. however, plan step 20's stop-condition nuance lists only steps 14\u201317 as the 'intentional-update list' and omits step 18. if the executor misses a call site and those tests fail, the stop condition text would classify the failure as 'unexpected' and trigger stop, even though the root cause is an incomplete step 18 execution. the stop condition should explicitly include step 18 (or enumerate `test_s2a_stamping.py` and `test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py`) so the executor has permission to fix residual failures in those files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "search-for-related-code-that-handles-the-same-concept-is-the-reported-issue-a-symptom-of-something-broader": [
    {

      "occurrence_count": 1,

    {
      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 10 claims `populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` reads 'db-populated fields' for `primary_topic_slug`, `participants_shape`, `read_scopes`, etc. however, the `bots` table ddl in the plan only defines `id`, `display_name`, and `created_at` \u2014 none of the botspec scope fields have db columns. in practice the function will hardcode mediator defaults (`primary_topic_slug='relationship'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`) and only read `display_name` from the db row. the plan's wording could mislead the executor into writing a `select * from bots` expecting columns that do not exist. the implementation should explicitly select only `display_name` and hardcode the rest, or the ddl should be expanded to store bot config. the architecture doc does not specify these columns on `bots`, so hardcoding is correct for s1, but the plan text should be clarified.",

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-010",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the locked brief \u00a716.1 work item 1 explicitly requires 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' seeded inside migration 0020. the revised plan instead moves channel seeding to a runtime script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. while the rationale (env vars differ across environments) is pragmatic, this is a concrete divergence from the locked architecture-doc brief which the plan must consume, not override. if the executor applies only the sql migrations, the `channels` table will be empty and routing will have no rows to resolve, breaking the mid-sprint checkpoint 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' gate. the runtime script must be documented as a mandatory post-migration step, but the brief expected it in the migration itself.",

    },

      ]
    },

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: eval-baseline-related criteria in the success_criteria list (criterion 13: 'eval baseline captured by writing scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py ...') will be unmeetable if t2 is deferred. plan should downgrade that criterion's priority from 'must' to 'should' or mark deferred.",

    "subsystem": "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements",

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-004",

        "occurrence_count": 1,

    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-011",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

        "id": "DEBT-019",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-026",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
        "occurrence_count": 1,

        ]
      },

)
            return "UPDATE 1"
        if compact.startswith("UPDATE themes SET status = 'dormant'"):
            now = args[0]
            for theme in self.themes.values():
                last = theme.get("last_reinforced_at") or theme.get("first_seen_at")
                if theme.get("status") == "active" and last is not None and last <= now - timedelta(weeks=6):
                    theme["status"] = "dormant"
                    theme["updated_at"] = now
            return "UPDATE 1"
        if compact.startswith("UPDATE themes SET status = 'resolved_by_time'"):
            now = args[0]
            for theme in self.themes.values():
                if theme.get("status") == "dormant" and theme.get("updated_at") is not None and theme["updated_at"] <= now - timedelta(days=120):
                    theme["status"] = "resolved_by_time"
                    theme["updated_at"] = now
            return "UPDATE 1"
        if compact.startswith("UPDATE observations SET status = 'stale'"):
            now = args[0]
            for observation in self.observations.values():
                last = observation.get("last_reinforced_at") or observation.get("created_at")
                if observation.get("status") == "active" and last is not None and last <= now - timedelta(days=183):
                    observation["status"] = "stale"
            return "UPDATE 1"
        if compact.startswith("UPDATE observations SET confidence = CASE confidence"):
            now = args[0]
            for observation in self.observations.values():
                last = observation.get("last_reinforced_at") or observation.get("created_at")
                if (
                    observation.get("status") == "active"
                    and last is not None
                    and last <= now - timedelta(days=91)
                    and last > now - timedelta(days=183)
                    and observation.get("confidence") in {"high", "medium"}
                ):
                    observation["confidence"] = "medium" if observation["confidence"] == "high" else "low"
            return "UPDATE 1"
        if compact.startswith("UPDATE watch_items SET status = 'expired'"):
            now = args[0]
            for item in self.watch_items.values():
                if (
                    item.get("status") == "open"
                    and item.get("due_at") is not None
                    and item.get("addressed_at") is None
                    and item["due_at"] <= now - timedelta(days=30)
                ):
                    item["status"] = "expired"
            return "UPDATE 1"

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — artifact_topics JOIN cutover for reads

## Overview

S3 cuts every artifact **read** in `memories | themes | observations | watch_items | distillations | out_of_bounds` over to a `JOIN artifact_topics` filtered by `topic_id = <bind> AND status = 'active'`. Behavior is identical at launch (mediator's topic is always `relationship`).

**v4 pivot (single high-leverage fix).** v3 mandated prepend-and-shift parameter binding (topic_id at `$1`, all other params renumbered). The gate verified that this convention breaks FakePool's positional `args[0]/args[1]` indexing across ~10 matcher bodies in `tests/conftest.py` — and the brief never mandated prepend. v4 switches to **append-last**: topic_id is bound at the final `$N` in every rewritten query. Existing positional args keep their indices, so every `args[0]`/`args[1]` body in FakePool continues to read the expected value. This single change resolves `correctness-1`, `correctness-2`, `correctness-3`, `FLAG-012`, `issue_hints`, `all_locations`, and `callers` simultaneously, with a much smaller production diff than v3.

Two further targeted additions close the remaining flags:
- **Step 4 (extended)**: add a small `self.artifact_topics: dict[tuple[str, UUID], UUID]` data structure to FakePool with helpers, and extend the decay UPDATE handlers at `conftest.py:2115-2160` to consult it. Makes the new decay scope test (Step 12) and the property-test count safeguard (Step 9) actually meaningful.
- **Step 4 (clarified)**: still need to relax the three `startswith` matchers at `conftest.py:1403/1406/1409` to substring form because the JOIN inserts a `t/m/o`-alias token between `FROM <table>` and `WHERE id = ANY` — but matcher BODIES are now untouched.

### Locked decisions (carried forward; no relitigation)
1. Helper at `app/services/topic_filter.py::join_artifact_topics(table_alias, topic_id_param)`. `topic_id_param` has no default — caller passes explicit `$N`.
2. Decay is per-topic; module-level rationale comment required.
3. OOB delivery-time guardrail filters via `artifact_topics.topic_id = <bind>`.
4. Mediator primary topic = `relationship`; defaulted into function bodies via `get_relationship_topic_id()`.
5. No multi-topic writes (S6); no scope enforcement (S4); no `scope` arg on read tools (S4).
6. Migration `0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql` ships, adding partial index `(topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active'`.
7. `scoring.py::rescore_observations` scoped same as decay.
8. Write-tool linked-ID validations get the JOIN: `write_tools.py:889`, `:1119` (CTE pre-check), `:1246`, `_require_existing_distillation_links`. UPDATEs in `write_tools.py` stay direct (S6).
9. `app/routers/admin.py` stays global; whitelisted in lint with `# allowlisted: admin operator view`.

### Constraints
- Off-limits: `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`, `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`.
- `tests/conftest.py` and `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py` are explicitly NOT off-limits.
- Allowed dirty files (query-rewrite only): `app/services/hot_context.py`, `prompts.py`, `time_context.py`.

### Caller enumeration (verified by grep)
- `build_hot_context`: `app/services/agentic.py:719` (has `ctx`), `app/staging.py:46` (uses default), `tests/test_hot_context.py` × 8 at 255/316/338/389/428/461/491/503 (off-limits; survive via Step 4 registry bootstrap).
- `check_oob_with_policy`: `app/services/hooks.py:22`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:752-760`.
- `summarize_partner_oob`: `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:763-765`, `tests/test_oob_countersummary.py:52/65/81`.
- `run_decay_housekeeping`: `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `tests/test_decay.py:93`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:347` (monkeypatched; `fake_decay` widened in Step 11).
- `rescore_observations`: `app/services/decay.py:94` only.

## Phase 1: Helper, plumbing, test infra, preflight

### Step 1: Preflight + read-path partial index (`scripts/`, `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Verify** `SELECT count(*) FROM mediator.artifact_topics WHERE status='active'` (≥235). asyncpg `statement_cache_size=0`.
2. **Backfill-completeness gate**: per-table `NOT EXISTS` check. STOP if any non-zero.
3. **Create** `migrations/0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql`:
   ```sql
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_topic_artifact_active
       ON artifact_topics (topic_id, artifact_table)
       WHERE status = 'active';
   ```
   Apply via psql port 5432 session-mode.

### Step 2: Topic-filter helper (`app/services/topic_filter.py`, `tests/test_topic_filter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Six-alias map (`m/t/o/w/d/x`). `topic_id_param` has NO default; `ValueError` on unknown alias. Emits JOIN fragment using `_at_<alias>` prefix (lint recognition signal).
2. Unit test confirms shape + `ValueError`.

### Step 3: Defaulted-kwarg plumbing (`app/services/hot_context.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, `app/services/oob_check.py`, `app/services/scoring.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add keyword-only `topic_id: UUID | None = None` (`primary_topic_id` on `build_hot_context`) to each entry point. Resolve `topic = topic_id or get_relationship_topic_id()`. Raise `RuntimeError("primary_topic_id unavailable: registry not populated")` only if both None.
2. **Production callers pass explicitly** (the default is a safety net, not load-bearing):
   - `agentic.py` → `primary_topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `read_tools.py:752-760` → `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `read_tools.py:763-765` → `topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.
   - `hooks.py:22` → `topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()` (no `ctx`).
   - `scheduled_job_handlers.py` decay invocation → **do NOT** pass `topic_id=` (in-function default handles it; avoids the `fake_decay` TypeError path).
   - `staging.py:46` → `primary_topic_id=get_relationship_topic_id()`.

### Step 4: Test infra (`tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_registry_bootstrap.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (closes `correctness-1/2/3/4/5`, `FLAG-009`, `FLAG-012`, `FLAG-013`, `all_locations-1/2/3/4/5`, `callers-1/2/3/4`, `issue_hints`, `issue_hints-1/2`, `all_locations`, `callers`).

1. **Registry bootstrap** — session-scoped autouse fixture seeds `app.bots.registry._RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID` with `UUID("00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001")` before any test runs. Makes `get_relationship_topic_id()` return non-None without app startup.
2. **artifact_topics data structure on FakePool** — add `self.artifact_topics: dict[tuple[str, UUID], UUID] = {}` to the FakePool `__init__`, keyed by `(artifact_table, artifact_id)` with value `topic_id`. Add a helper method:
   ```python
   def link_topic(self, artifact_table: str, artifact_id, topic_id):
       self.artifact_topics[(artifact_table, artifact_id)] = topic_id
   ```
   Existing fixtures that seed artifacts can opt in to scope-aware behavior by calling `link_topic` (the property test in Step 9 and the new decay test in Step 12 use this). Existing tests that DON'T call `link_topic` see the same global behavior as before — backwards compatible.
3. **Matcher signature relaxation** — at `conftest.py:1403/1406/1409`, convert `compact.startswith("SELECT id FROM <table> WHERE id = ANY")` to `"FROM <table>" in compact and "WHERE id = ANY" in compact`. This is needed because Step 5/6's rewritten query inserts a `t/m/o`-alias token (e.g. `FROM themes t JOIN artifact_topics ...`), no longer matching the `startswith` prefix. **Bodies of these matchers are NOT changed** — append-last binding (Step 5/6) preserves positional args, so `args[0]` continues to be the ID array as before.
4. **Substring matchers unchanged** — at `:1217, :1369, :1371, :1480, :1501, :1517, :1533, :1560`, the `'FROM <table>' in compact` matchers still match because the artifact-table name is preserved in the rewritten SQL. Bodies untouched. Add ONE comment near these matchers stating: "JOIN artifact_topics filter is honored at the FakePool level via self.artifact_topics; matchers below ignore the JOIN clause."
5. **Decay UPDATE handler extension** — at `conftest.py:2115-2160` (the FakePool decay UPDATE handlers for themes/observations/watch_items), add scope filtering. Pseudocode addition:
   ```python
   # When an UPDATE includes "FROM artifact_topics at" and a topic_id param,
   # skip rows whose (table, id) -> topic in self.artifact_topics does not
   # match the supplied topic_id. If a row has no artifact_topics entry,
   # keep current behavior (global update) for backwards compatibility.
   ```
   Implementation: inspect the SQL `compact` for `"FROM artifact_topics" in compact`; if present, extract the topic_id param (the trailing `$N` because of append-last) and filter row iteration. Single helper `_row_matches_topic(table, row_id, topic_id) -> bool` reused across the three UPDATE handlers.
6. **Sanity tests** — `tests/test_conftest_registry_bootstrap.py` asserts `get_relationship_topic_id()` returns the seeded UUID and that `FakePool.link_topic` + `_row_matches_topic` behave as documented (one positive, one negative). Two tests, <1s.

## Phase 2: Read-site rewrites (append-last convention)

### Step 5: Rewrite `hot_context.py` reads (`app/services/hot_context.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Convention** (v4 update): **append** `topic_id` to the params list as the FINAL bind, then pass `f"${len(params)}"` to the helper. Existing positional params keep their `$N` indices intact. No renumbering anywhere in the query. This is what FakePool's bodies expect.
2. Rewrite the six SELECTs (out_of_bounds :227-235, memories :247-258, themes :273-282, watch_items :292-300, observations :315-327, distillations :344-360).
3. Preserve all `ORDER BY`, `LIMIT`, `WHERE` clauses (including `OR about_user_id IS NULL`).

### Step 6: Rewrite `read_tools.py` reads (`app/services/tools/read_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Same append-last convention: `params.append(ctx.primary_topic_id)` after all existing params; pass `f"${len(params)}"` to the helper.
2. Sites: `list_themes` (:520-533), `get_theme` (:537-547), related-memory at :549-552, related-observation at :553-556, `get_memories` (:582-592), `list_watch_items` (:609-620), `get_observations` (:636-651), `get_distillations` (:682-702), `get_oob` (:739-749).
3. Keep all secondary filters.

### Step 7: `oob_check.py`; audit-clean `cross_thread_privacy.py` (`app/services/oob_check.py`, `app/services/cross_thread_privacy.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `_active_oob_entries` at `:112-133` — add `topic_id: UUID` param, append-last binding, helper-JOIN (alias `x`).
2. `cross_thread_privacy.py` — no direct artifact reads; audit clean.

### Step 8: `scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `decay.py`, `scoring.py`; audit-clean `withheld_reviews.py`
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`scheduled_job_handlers.py`**: weekly-summary subqueries at `:248-266` — helper-JOIN, topic from `job.get('topic_id') or get_relationship_topic_id()`, appended last. `_fetch_watch_item` at `:272-283` — add `topic_id` param, helper-JOIN, append-last.
2. **`decay.py`**: add the top-of-module per-topic policy comment. Rewrite the five UPDATEs:
   ```sql
   UPDATE themes
   SET status='dormant', updated_at = $1
   FROM artifact_topics at
   WHERE at.artifact_table = 'themes'
     AND at.artifact_id = themes.id
     AND at.topic_id = $2
     AND at.status = 'active'
     AND themes.status = 'active'
     AND COALESCE(themes.last_reinforced_at, themes.first_seen_at) <= $1::timestamptz - interval '6 weeks'
   ```
   `$1` is `now`, `$2` is the resolved topic_id (append-last after the existing `now` param). Same shape for the other UPDATEs. The lint regex `\bFROM\s+(memories|themes|...)` does NOT match `FROM artifact_topics`, so no whitelist needed.
3. **`scoring.py::rescore_observations`** at `:125-165` — add `topic_id: UUID | None = None`, default-resolution, helper-JOIN the SELECT (alias `o`, topic appended last). UPDATE is keyed by id post-filter; no change.
4. **`withheld_reviews.py`** — INSERTs only. Audit clean.

## Phase 3: Tests, lint, validation

### Step 9: Property/frozen-fixture test (`tests/test_hot_context_join_cutover.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. New file (off-limits rule on `test_hot_context.py`).
2. Fixture seeds one each of the six artifact families. For each seeded artifact, call `pool.link_topic('<table>', artifact_id, RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC)` (Step 4.2 helper). This makes the JOIN meaningful at the FakePool level — drop a `link_topic` call for any artifact and that artifact will disappear from the rendered hot context (count safeguard fires).
3. **Snapshot**: capture rendered hot context from S2b tip in a scratch worktree, embed as `_S2B_RENDERED_HOT_CONTEXT`. Assert byte-equality.
4. **Drift safeguard**: assert each list length equals seeded count. With Step 4.2 in place, this safeguard is meaningful — a missing `link_topic` row drops the artifact and trips the length assertion.
5. Add `test_build_hot_context_raises_when_topic_registry_unset` — monkeypatch `get_relationship_topic_id` to None, call without kwarg, assert `RuntimeError`.

### Step 10: EXPLAIN regression script (`scripts/explain_artifact_topics_reads.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. `--help` parses. asyncpg `statement_cache_size=0`; schema `mediator`.
2. Six representative queries (one per artifact table).
3. PASS = `Index Scan` / `Index Only Scan` / `Bitmap Index Scan` on the new partial index. WARN on the older index. FAIL on `Seq Scan`. Non-zero exit on FAIL.

### Step 11: Read-side lint + fake_decay widening + admin comments (`scripts/lint_artifact_reads.py`, `tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py`, `app/routers/admin.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Lint** — `scripts/lint_artifact_reads.py` scans `app/` for `\bFROM\s+(memories|themes|observations|watch_items|distillations|out_of_bounds)\b`. For each hit, checks that the enclosing SQL also contains `_at_` (helper-emitted alias prefix). Whitelist: `app/services/topic_filter.py`, `tests/`, `migrations/`, the lint itself, `app/routers/admin.py`. Docstring records FROM-only design + S6 TODO for UPDATEs + clarification that `FROM artifact_topics` (decay's UPDATE shape) is not in the regex alternation and therefore not flagged.
2. CLI: `--help`, `--dir`.
3. Wire as pytest: `tests/test_lint_artifact_reads.py::test_lint_artifact_reads_clean` (subprocess, exit 0).
4. **admin.py allowlist comments** at `:177, :182, :192, :202, :216, :268`: `# allowlisted: admin operator view`.
5. **fake_decay widening** — `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:343` from `async def fake_decay(pool):` to `async def fake_decay(pool, **kwargs):`. Belt-and-braces with Step 3.2's "don't pass `topic_id=` at the call site" decision.

### Step 12: Decay per-topic tests (`tests/test_decay.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Pre-existing `run_decay_housekeeping(fake_pool, now=now)` at `:93` survives via Step 4 registry bootstrap.
2. Add `test_decay_only_touches_rows_for_current_topic`: seed two themes/observations/watch_items pairs, call `pool.link_topic(...)` to link one of each to `RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC` and the other to a synthetic second topic UUID. Call `run_decay_housekeeping(pool, now=...)` (default → relationship). Assert mutation only on relationship-linked rows — Step 4.5's decay UPDATE handler extension makes the FakePool enforce this.
3. Add `test_decay_scopes_when_explicit_topic_passed`: call with `topic_id=other_topic_id`; assert the inverse.

### Step 13: Write-tool linked-ID validation rewrites (`app/services/tools/write_tools.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `:889` `SELECT owner_user_id FROM watch_items WHERE id=$1` → helper-JOIN, append-last topic at `$2`.
2. `:1119` CTE in `revise_distillation` — sibling topic-scoped existence pre-check before the CTE; raise `ToolCallRejected` if 0.
3. `:1246` `SELECT owner_id FROM out_of_bounds WHERE id=$1` → helper-JOIN, append-last topic at `$2`.
4. `_require_existing_distillation_links` near `:1100` — helper-JOIN each existence SELECT (append-last).
5. Only add `tests/test_write_tools_topic_scoping.py` if existing non-off-limits coverage misses the path.

## Phase 4: Validation

### Step 14: Targeted test suite
**Scope:** Small
1. Cheap first: `test_topic_filter`, `test_conftest_registry_bootstrap`, `test_decay`, `test_hot_context_join_cutover`, `test_lint_artifact_reads`, `test_oob_countersummary`, `test_scheduled_jobs`.
2. Broader: `pytest tests/ -x`. Off-limits files (`test_hot_context.py`, `test_tools.py`) MUST keep passing untouched.
3. Lint scripts: `--help`, then run on `app/`, both exit 0.

### Step 15: EXPLAIN + manual snapshot
**Scope:** Small
1. `python scripts/explain_artifact_topics_reads.py --help`; run against prod; PASS on all six.
2. Eval baseline skipped per brief.

## Execution Order
1. Preflight + new index (1). Helper (2). Plumbing (3). Test infra (4).
2. Read rewrites with append-last convention: `hot_context.py` (5), `read_tools.py` (6), `oob_check.py` (7), `scheduled_job_handlers.py`+`decay.py`+`scoring.py` (8).
3. Property test (9), EXPLAIN (10), lint+fake_decay+admin comments (11), decay scope tests (12), write-tool validations (13).
4. Validation (14, 15).

## Validation Order
1. Backfill-completeness preflight (Step 1.2) — STOPs on non-zero.
2. Unit-level: helper, registry bootstrap, decay scope, hot_context_join_cutover, lint.
3. Full `pytest tests/ -x`. Off-limits tests must keep passing.
4. Both lint scripts exit zero.
5. Property test (frozen-fixture + count-equality safeguard, now meaningful via Step 4.2).
6. EXPLAIN against prod.

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },

      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: routing unit tests require extending fakepool with `channels`, `user_identities`, `bot_bindings`, `dyads`, and `dyad_members` dicts so the sql patterns in `resolve_bot`, `resolve_sender`, and `resolve_binding` can match against stored rows. however, `tests/conftest.py` is explicitly listed in the brief as a dirty file that must not be touched. the revised plan says \"extend fakepool minimally\" twice (step 9 and step 11 assumption) but never specifies how to do so without modifying `conftest.py`\u2014for example by defining a local `routingfakepool(fakepool)` subclass inside `tests/test_routing.py` or by monkeypatching the class in that test module. if the executor follows the plan literally and edits `conftest.py`, they will violate a locked constraint. if they do not extend fakepool, the routing unit tests cannot be implemented as written. this is a supporting-infrastructure gap.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-015",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: step 3 updates scripts/seed_channels.py to import _decode_discord_user_id from the new shared module. the seeder also previously had a regex `_discord_bot_user_id_re` constant (or similar) inline; if the seeder's local regex remains while the new module redefines it, the two could drift. plan should explicitly say the seeder deletes its local copy.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-016",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: plan step 14.3 lists hooks.py emitters but `paused_for_user` does not currently call `logger.*` (verified by reading hooks.py). the plan instructs to log `extra={user_id, bot_id}` \u2014 if there is no existing log call, the executor must add one, which is a behavior change rather than an annotation. minor scope creep.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-023",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py is still listed as a deliverable (step 2.1) and a smoke test (step 2.7 `--help` invocation). if t2 is deferred, the script could either (a) not be written, or (b) be written but its run deferred. plan should specify which. the smoke test alone (`--help`) does not require the pooler.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-024",
      "concern": "does the change touch all locations and supporting infrastructure?: phase 0 contains both step 1 (parity + identity seeding pre-flight) and step 2 (eval baseline). step 1 is independent of the pooler block \u2014 it queries via `psql` or any working connection \u2014 and should not be deferred. plan should make clear step 1 still gates phase 1, but step 2 is parked.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    }
  ],
  "find-the-callers-of-the-changed-function-what-arguments-do-they-actually-pass-does-the-fix-handle-all-of-them": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: new issue: the 0020 migration ddl defines `channels` with `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)`. postgresql treats null != null under standard unique constraints, so two whatsapp rows with `guild_id=null, channel_id=null` and the same transport+address would not conflict. however, `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, which requires a matching unique index on those coalesce expressions. without it, the seed script will raise 'there is no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification'. the ddl should either use `unique nulls not distinct` (pg15+, and the repo uses pg16 per `test_eval_workflow.py:36`) or a unique index on the coalesce expressions.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-017",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: step 13.2 adds `bot_id` kwarg to hooks.paused_for_user. callers grep shows it is called from various places; the plan only enumerates messaging.py:170,335 as new bot_id-passing sites. other callers continue to call without bot_id (none default), which is fine, but plan should call out that all existing callers continue to work without modification.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-025",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: evals.runner.run_scenarios is referenced by the deferred step 2.1; plan doesn't enumerate that runner's callers or its schema dependencies. since step 2 is being deferred, this is moot \u2014 but if the script is still authored as a step 2.1 deliverable, it must not import anything that hits the pooler at import time.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-033",
      "concern": "find the callers of the changed function. what arguments do they actually pass? does the fix handle all of them?: plan step 18 strips `bot_spec` defaults from `tests/_scope_helpers.py:make_mediator_ctx`, which has six call sites across `tests/test_s2a_stamping.py` (5 calls) and `tests/test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py` (1 call) that rely on the implicit default. the executor must update all six call sites to pass `bot_spec` explicitly. however, plan step 20's stop-condition nuance lists only steps 14\u201317 as the 'intentional-update list' and omits step 18. if the executor misses a call site and those tests fail, the stop condition text would classify the failure as 'unexpected' and trigger stop, even though the root cause is an incomplete step 18 execution. the stop condition should explicitly include step 18 (or enumerate `test_s2a_stamping.py` and `test_s2a_artifact_topics_same_stmt.py`) so the executor has permission to fix residual failures in those files.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [

      ]
    }

      "id": "DEBT-007",
      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: step 10 claims `populate_mediator_spec_from_db(pool)` reads 'db-populated fields' for `primary_topic_slug`, `participants_shape`, `read_scopes`, etc. however, the `bots` table ddl in the plan only defines `id`, `display_name`, and `created_at` \u2014 none of the botspec scope fields have db columns. in practice the function will hardcode mediator defaults (`primary_topic_slug='relationship'`, `participants_shape='dyad'`) and only read `display_name` from the db row. the plan's wording could mislead the executor into writing a `select * from bots` expecting columns that do not exist. the implementation should explicitly select only `display_name` and hardcode the rest, or the ddl should be expanded to store bot config. the architecture doc does not specify these columns on `bots`, so hardcoding is correct for s1, but the plan text should be clarified.",

      "concern": "search for related code that handles the same concept. is the reported issue a symptom of something broader?: the locked brief \u00a716.1 work item 1 explicitly requires 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' seeded inside migration 0020. the revised plan instead moves channel seeding to a runtime script `scripts/seed_channels.py`. while the rationale (env vars differ across environments) is pragmatic, this is a concrete divergence from the locked architecture-doc brief which the plan must consume, not override. if the executor applies only the sql migrations, the `channels` table will be empty and routing will have no rows to resolve, breaking the mid-sprint checkpoint 'channel rows for current discord + whatsapp' gate. the runtime script must be documented as a mandatory post-migration step, but the brief expected it in the migration itself.",

      {
        "id": "DEBT-001",
        "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

    "subsystem": "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct",
    "total_occurrences": 5,
    "plan_count": 3,
    "entries": [
      {
        "id": "DEBT-002",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },
      {
        "id": "DEBT-011",
        "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
        "occurrence_count": 1,
        "plan_ids": [
          "sprint-1-foundation"
        ]
      },

)

    async def _weekly_summary_counts(self, user_id: UUID) -> dict[str, int]:
        row = await self.pool.fetchrow(
            """
            SELECT
                (
                    SELECT COUNT(*)
                    FROM messages
                    WHERE deleted_at IS NULL
                      AND sent_at >= now() - interval '7 days'
                      AND (sender_id = $1 OR recipient_id = $1)
                )::int AS conversation_count,
                (
                    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM themes WHERE status = 'active')
                    +
                    (SELECT COUNT(*) FROM watch_items WHERE owner_user_id = $1 AND status = 'open')
                )::int AS ongoing_count
            """,
            user_id,
        )
        return {
            "conversation_count": int(row["conversation_count"] or 0),
            "ongoing_count": int(row["ongoing_count"] or 0),
        }

    async def _fetch_watch_item(self, watch_item_id: Any) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        if watch_item_id is None:
            return None
        row = await self.pool.fetchrow(
            """
            SELECT id, owner_user_id, content, due_at, status
            FROM watch_items
            WHERE id = $1
            """,
            watch_item_id,
        )
        return dict(row) if row is not None else None


def _user_from_row(row: dict[str, Any]) -> User:
    return User(id=row["id"], name=row["name"], phone=row["phone"], timezone=row["timezone"])


def _scheduled_task_trigger_context(job: dict[str, Any], context: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:

1. **CLI scripts**: invoke with `--help` to confirm parseable.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Sprint 3 — artifact_topics JOIN cutover for reads

## Overview

S3 cuts every artifact **read** in `memories | themes | observations | watch_items | distillations | out_of_bounds` over to `JOIN artifact_topics` filtered by `topic_id = <bind> AND status = 'active'`. Behavior is identical at launch (mediator's topic is always `relationship`).

**v5 surgical revision.** v4's structure (append-last binding, registry-bootstrap fixture, `artifact_topics` dict on FakePool, decay UPDATE handler extension) is sound. The only remaining gap is that Step 4.3 enumerated three `startswith` matcher relaxations in conftest.py but missed three more at lines `:1217, :1369, :1371` that target SQL the plan rewrites in Step 8.1 (`_fetch_watch_item`) and Step 13 (write_tools.py:889/1246 linked-ID validations). Same class of fix — three additional line numbers. v5 adds them and nothing else, plus a one-line inline-comment note about the `_row_matches_topic` fallback hazard (correctness-3, accepted maintainability debt). Plan delta is intentionally tiny vs v4 (gate flagged 78.5% v4 delta as churn).

### Locked decisions (carried forward; no relitigation)
1. Helper at `app/services/topic_filter.py::join_artifact_topics(table_alias, topic_id_param)`. `topic_id_param` has no default; caller passes explicit `$N`.
2. Decay is per-topic; module-level rationale comment required.
3. OOB delivery-time guardrail filters via `artifact_topics.topic_id = <bind>`.
4. Mediator primary topic = `relationship`; defaulted via `get_relationship_topic_id()`.
5. No multi-topic writes (S6); no scope enforcement (S4); no `scope` arg on read tools (S4).
6. Migration `0030_artifact_topics_read_index.sql` ships, adding partial index `(topic_id, artifact_table) WHERE status='active'`.
7. `scoring.py::rescore_observations` scoped same as decay.
8. Write-tool linked-ID validations get the JOIN: `write_tools.py:889`, `:1119` (CTE pre-check), `:1246`, `_require_existing_distillation_links`. UPDATEs in `write_tools.py` stay direct (S6).
9. `app/routers/admin.py` stays global; whitelisted in lint with `# allowlisted: admin operator view`.
10. **Binding convention: append-last.** topic_id is bound at the final `$N` in every rewritten query; existing positional params keep their indices.

### Constraints
- Off-limits: `tests/test_hot_context.py`, `tests/test_time_context.py`, `tests/test_tools.py`, `tool_schemas.py`, `migrations/0019_feedback_reaction_context.sql`.
- `tests/conftest.py` and `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py` are explicitly NOT off-limits.
- Allowed dirty files (query-rewrite only): `app/services/hot_context.py`, `prompts.py`, `time_context.py`.

### Caller enumeration (verified by grep)
- `build_hot_context`: `app/services/agentic.py:719` (has `ctx`), `app/staging.py:46` (uses default), `tests/test_hot_context.py` × 8 (off-limits; survive via Step 4 registry bootstrap).
- `check_oob_with_policy`: `app/services/hooks.py:22`, `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:752-760`.
- `summarize_partner_oob`: `app/services/tools/read_tools.py:763-765`, `tests/test_oob_countersummary.py:52/65/81`.
- `run_decay_housekeeping`: `app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`, `tests/test_decay.py:93`, `tests/test_scheduled_jobs.py:347` (monkeypatched; `fake_decay` widened in Step 11).
- `rescore_observations`: `app/services/decay.py:94` only.

**Scope:** Small

   ```sql
   CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_artifact_topics_topic_artifact_active
       ON artifact_topics (topic_id, artifact_table)
       WHERE status = 'active';
   ```
   Apply via psql port 5432 session-mode.

### Step 2: Topic-filter helper (`app/services/topic_filter.py`, `tests/test_topic_filter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Six-alias map (`m/t/o/w/d/x`). `topic_id_param` has NO default; `ValueError` on unknown alias. Emits JOIN fragment using `_at_<alias>` prefix (lint recognition signal).
2. Unit test confirms shape + `ValueError`.

### Step 3: Defaulted-kwarg plumbing (`app/services/hot_context.py`, `app/services/decay.py`, `app/services/oob_check.py`, `app/services/scoring.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add keyword-only `topic_id: UUID | None = None` (`primary_topic_id` on `build_hot_context`) to each entry point. Resolve `topic = topic_id or get_relationship_topic_id()`; raise `RuntimeError` only if both None.

   - `agentic.py` → `primary_topic_id=ctx.primary_topic_id`.

### Step 4: Test infra (`tests/conftest.py`, `tests/test_conftest_registry_bootstrap.py`)
**Scope:** Medium (closes all remaining open flags: `correctness-1`, `correctness-2`, `issue_hints`, `all_locations`, `callers`, `FLAG-016`).

1. **Registry bootstrap** — session-scoped autouse fixture seeds `app.bots.registry._RELATIONSHIP_TOPIC_ID` with `UUID("00000000-0000-4000-8000-000000000001")`.
2. **artifact_topics data structure on FakePool** — add `self.artifact_topics: dict[tuple[str, UUID], UUID] = {}` and a `link_topic(artifact_table, artifact_id, topic_id)` helper. Tests that want scope-aware behavior call `link_topic`; tests that don't see backward-compatible global behavior.
3. **Matcher signature relaxation** (v5 expanded enumeration; closes `correctness-1`, `correctness-2`, `issue_hints`, `all_locations`, `callers`, `FLAG-016`). Convert each of these SIX `startswith` matchers from prefix form to substring form. Bodies UNTOUCHED — append-last preserves `args[0]` semantics in all cases.
   - **`:1217`** `compact.startswith("SELECT id, owner_user_id, content, due_at, status FROM watch_items WHERE id")` → `"FROM watch_items" in compact and "owner_user_id" in compact and "due_at" in compact and "WHERE id" in compact`. Used by `scheduled_job_handlers._fetch_watch_item` (Step 8.1 rewrite). The added substring discriminators (`owner_user_id`, `due_at`) are present in the rewritten SELECT column list and uniquely distinguish this matcher from `:1369` and `:1517` (the broader list_watch_items matcher).
   - **`:1369`** `compact.startswith("SELECT owner_user_id FROM watch_items WHERE id")` → `"SELECT owner_user_id" in compact and "FROM watch_items" in compact and "WHERE" in compact`. Used by `write_tools.py:889` (Step 13.1 rewrite). The `SELECT owner_user_id` prefix uniquely distinguishes this from `:1217`'s broader column list.
   - **`:1371`** `compact.startswith("SELECT owner_id FROM out_of_bounds WHERE id")` → `"SELECT owner_id" in compact and "FROM out_of_bounds" in compact and "WHERE" in compact`. Used by `write_tools.py:1246` (Step 13.3 rewrite).
   - **`:1403`** `compact.startswith("SELECT id FROM themes WHERE id = ANY")` → `"FROM themes" in compact and "WHERE id = ANY" in compact and "SELECT id" in compact[:25]`. Used by `read_tools.get_theme` related-theme lookup (Step 6).
   - **`:1406`** `compact.startswith("SELECT id FROM memories WHERE id = ANY")` → `"FROM memories" in compact and "WHERE id = ANY" in compact and "SELECT id" in compact[:25]`. Same shape; used by `read_tools.get_theme` related-memory lookup.
   - **`:1409`** `compact.startswith("SELECT id FROM observations WHERE id = ANY")` → `"FROM observations" in compact and "WHERE id = ANY" in compact and "SELECT id" in compact[:25]`. Same shape; used by `read_tools.get_theme` related-observation lookup.

   For each relaxation, verify during execute that the new substring set still uniquely disambiguates from the other matchers in the `FakePool.fetch`/`fetchrow` ordering. The `compact[:25]` slice on the three `:1403/:1406/:1409` matchers prevents false positives from queries that mention `SELECT id` deep inside a CTE.
4. **Substring matchers unchanged** — at `:1480, :1501, :1517, :1533, :1560`, the `"FROM <table>" in compact` matchers continue to match after the JOIN cutover because the artifact-table name is preserved in the rewritten SQL. Bodies untouched. Add ONE comment near these matchers: "JOIN artifact_topics filter is honored at the FakePool level via self.artifact_topics; matchers below ignore the JOIN clause."
5. **Decay UPDATE handler extension** — at `conftest.py:2115-2160`, add a `_row_matches_topic(table, row_id, topic_id) -> bool` helper consulted when the SQL contains `"FROM artifact_topics"`. Rows without an `artifact_topics` entry retain global update behavior (backward compatibility). **Note** (closes `correctness-3` as accepted debt): add an inline comment at the helper warning that tests authoring new decay-touched artifacts must call `pool.link_topic(...)` if they want scope enforcement — otherwise the row is treated as global. Phrasing: `# Backward-compat fallback: rows without a link_topic entry update globally. New tests asserting per-topic decay MUST call pool.link_topic for every seeded artifact, or the scoping bug they're checking will silently pass.`
6. **Sanity tests** — `tests/test_conftest_registry_bootstrap.py` (2 tests):
   - `test_relationship_topic_seeded`: `get_relationship_topic_id()` returns the seeded UUID.
   - `test_link_topic_and_row_matches`: `pool.link_topic('themes', X, T1)`; assert `_row_matches_topic('themes', X, T1) is True` and `_row_matches_topic('themes', X, T2) is False`; for an unlinked row Y, assert the helper returns True (backward-compat fallback).

        Known accepted debt grouped by subsystem:
        {
  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-002",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan\u2019s `scripts/seed_channels.py` uses `on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing`, but the 0020 migration defines the table with a standard `unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id)` constraint on raw columns. postgresql `on conflict` target lists must reference the exact column names of a unique constraint or a matching unique index; expression constructs like `coalesce` in the target list do not match a raw-column unique constraint and will raise a syntax or 'no unique or exclusion constraint matching the on conflict specification' error at runtime. the seed script will fail unless either (a) the table is changed to use an expression-based unique index with those exact coalesce expressions, or (b) the seed script is rewritten to query-first/insert-if-missing or uses the plain column names and accepts the null-handling semantics of the constraint.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-011",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: phase b cte pair (`ins` and `src`) requires identical where/order/limit clauses for cursor correctness. both are written aligned but nothing enforces the invariant during executor edits; drift would silently mis-advance cursor.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-1-foundation"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-019",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: step 5.1 says `dyad_id` is appended `before the existing-fields preserved block beginning at line 34`. actual line 34 in turn_context.py is a blank/comment line followed by `current_step: turnstep = \"respond\"` at the next line. placement intent is clear (append at end of optional block), but the line reference may drift after the field is added. minor precision wobble; not a blocker.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-026",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: eval baseline capture (step 2) is technically blocked by infra. the plan's approach (write script in main tree, copy to worktree, run there) assumes `python scripts/capture_eval_baseline.py` can connect to the supabase pooler. per user note, the pooler's transaction-mode does not preserve `set search_path` across connection acquisitions, so the script would fail with schema lookup errors. the plan should add an alternative path (port 5432 session-mode override, or explicit schema-qualified queries everywhere, or defer entirely).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2a-stamp-rekey"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-031",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: the lint script `scripts/lint_inserts.py` has a directory-not-found error path at lines 305-309 that still exits 0. the plan step 13 says to remove the `# todo(s2b)` marker at line 308 but does not mention changing the `sys.exit(0)` at line 309 to a non-zero exit code. after the script is flipped to blocking mode, invoking it with an invalid `--dir` argument (or when `args.dir` resolves to a non-existent path) will silently exit 0. this defeats the ci-blocking intent because a typo in the scan directory would be treated as success instead of failure. the fix is trivial: change `sys.exit(0)` to `sys.exit(1)` at line 309 (or remove the error-path todo and update the exit code in the same step).",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "sprint-2b-constraints"
      ]
    }
  ],
  "did-the-work-fully-address-the-issue-hints-user-notes-and-approved-plan-requirements": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-001",
      "concern": "did the work fully address the issue hints, user notes, and approved plan requirements?: the scripts/seed_channels.py plan uses on conflict (transport, address, coalesce(guild_id,''), coalesce(channel_id,'')) do nothing, but the 0020 migration creates a plain unique (transport, address, guild_id, channel_id) constraint. postgresql requires the on conflict target to match a unique constraint or index exactly; an expression-based target without a matching expression index will raise a runtime error. additionally, the raw unique constraint treats nulls as distinct, so it cannot deduplicate whatsapp channels where guild_id and channel_id are null. the executor must either add a matching unique expression index in 0020, or rewrite the seed script to use a pre-insert select check instead of on conflict.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,

        Debt guidance:
        - These are known accepted limitations. Do not re-flag them unless the current plan makes them worse, broadens them, or fails to contain them.
        - Prefix every new concern with a subsystem tag followed by a colon, for example `Timeout recovery: retry backoff remains brittle`.
        - When a concern is recurring debt that still needs to be flagged, prefix it with `Recurring debt:` after the subsystem tag, for example `Timeout recovery: Recurring debt: retry backoff remains brittle`.

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.
